divisible, and $\gap_\tau=0$ if and only if $\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$ is coinitial in $\Delta^{>0}$.
The sum $\tau(\Gamma)+\gap_\tau$ is direct and ordered lexicographically with $\tau(\Gamma)$
outer: $h<h'$ in $\tau(\Gamma)$ and $c,c'\in\gap_\tau$ give $h+c<h'+c'$. Hence a well-ordered
family of well-ordered blocks lying in distinct cosets $h+\gap_\tau$ has well-ordered union.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $c,c'\in\gap_\tau$ and $0<h\in\tau(\Gamma)$, $|c+c'|<h/2+h/2$; symmetry and convexity are
clear, and $0<c\in\tau(\Gamma)$ fails the condition at $h=c$. If $D$ is convex with
$D\cap\tau(\Gamma)=0$, then $h\le n|c|$ with $c\in D$ would put $h\in D$, so $D\subseteq\gap_\tau$.
Convex subgroups of divisible groups are divisible. If $\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$ is coinitial no
$c>0$ lies in $\gap_\tau$; otherwise some $c>0$ lies below every $h\in\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$, hence
below every $h/n$. For the blocks, $h'-h>|c-c'|$; a nonempty subset of a union of blocks has a
least outer coordinate and a least element in that block.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Bottom-gap classification \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:classification}
There is a bijection between
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},itemsep=1pt]
\item strongly additive field embeddings $J:K_\Gamma\hookrightarrow K_\Delta$ with
$J(t^\gamma)=t^{\tau(\gamma)}$ for all $\gamma$ and $J(\mathcal R_\Gamma)\subseteq\mathcal R_\Delta$;
and
\item ordered field embeddings $\rho:\R\hookrightarrow\Hahn\R{\gap_\tau}$,
\end{enumerate}
given by $\rho=J|_\R$ and
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:classification}
 J_{\tau,\rho}\Bigl(\sum_\gamma r_\gamma t^\gamma\Bigr)=\sum_\gamma\rho(r_\gamma)\,t^{\tau(\gamma)} .
\end{equation}
Every $J=J_{\tau,\rho}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:classprops}
 \begin{gathered}
 v(Jx)=\tau(v(x)),\qquad \lc(Jx)=\lc(x),\qquad \ct(Jx)=\ct(x),\\
 J^{-1}(\mathcal R_\Delta)=\mathcal R_\Gamma,\qquad J^{-1}(\Pi_\Delta)=\Pi_\Gamma .
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Without strongness, every field embedding with $J(t^\gamma)=t^{\tau(\gamma)}$ and
$J(\mathcal R_\Gamma)\subseteq\mathcal R_\Delta$ still has $J(\R)\subseteq\Hahn\R{\gap_\tau}$.
\end{theorem}
Here $\ct(Jx)=\ct(x)$ is an equality of coefficient extractions, not multiplicativity of $\ct$.

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1: residues.} An ordered embedding of $\R$ into a real Hahn field fixes $\Q$; a
rational bound on $|r|$ bounds $|J(r)|$, so $J(r)$ has nonnegative support, and its constant term
lies between all rational bounds of $r$. So $J(r)=r+{}$(a series with positive support), with
first exponent $0$ and first coefficient $r$ when $r\ne0$. (An embedding of a real closed field
into an ordered field preserves positivity, positive elements being squares; so every field
embedding here is ordered.)

\emph{Step 2: the gap is necessary.} For $\gamma>0$ and $r\in\R$, $rt^{-\gamma}\in\Pi_\Gamma$, so
$J(r)t^{-\tau(\gamma)}\in\Pi_\Delta$ by the existential test. Hence every $c\in\supp J(r)$ has
$c<\tau(\gamma)$ for all $\gamma>0$; with $c\ge0$ and divisibility, $c\in\gap_\tau$. No strongness
is used. If $J$ is strong, expanding $x$ into its summable family of terms gives
\eqref{opa:as:eq:classification}, hence uniqueness.

\emph{Step 3: every $\rho$ is admissible.} For a support $S$, the blocks
$\tau(\gamma)+\supp\rho(r_\gamma)$, $\gamma\in S$, lie in distinct cosets of $\gap_\tau$ in the order
of $S$ (\cref{opa:as:lem:gap}), so \eqref{opa:as:eq:classification} is a Hahn series. For a summable
family $(x_j)$, the outer exponents form a well-ordered set, finitely many $j$ contribute at each
outer exponent, and each block is a finite union of well-ordered supports; so the images are
summable, and additivity of $\rho$ gives strong additivity.

\emph{Step 4: field operations.} $J(1)=1$. Products of terms map to
$\rho(r)t^{\tau(\gamma)}\rho(s)t^{\tau(\eta)}=\rho(rs)t^{\tau(\gamma+\eta)}$, and the product family is
summable (the support principle of \cref{opa:sec:prelim}); regrouping by Step 3 gives
multiplicativity. The first block of $J(x)$ starts at $\tau(v(x))$ with coefficient $\lc(x)$, which
gives injectivity, the order and the valuation formula.

\emph{Step 5: preservation and reflection.} For $\gamma<0$ the block at $\tau(\gamma)$ is negative, for
$\gamma>0$ positive; the zero block is $\rho(r_0)$ with constant term $r_0$; blocks cannot cancel. So
$\ct(Jx)=\ct(x)$. If $x\in\mathcal R_\Gamma$ its zero block is the integer $r_0$ and all other
blocks are negative. Conversely, if $J(x)\in\mathcal R_\Delta$, every positive block is absent and
$r_0\in\Z$, so $x\in\mathcal R_\Gamma$; for $\Pi$ also $r_0=0$.
\end{proof}

So these embeddings automatically \emph{reflect} the integer part and $\Pi$. The classification is
inside the exact-monomial category. It does not cover maps $t^\gamma\mapsto
c_\gamma t^{\tau(\gamma)}(1+u_\gamma)$ with characters or unit corrections, which already occur among
the automorphisms of \cref{opa:part:aut}, and it does not assert that omnific-preserving embeddings
are strong; beyond the category it gives only the necessary coefficient restriction of Step 2
\src{11} (\cref{opa:as:q:units,opa:as:q:embeddingstrong}).

\begin{corollary}[Surjectivity \src{11}]\label{opa:as:cor:onto}
$J_{\tau,\rho}$ is onto if and only if $\tau$ is onto. Then $\gap_\tau=0$, $\rho=\id_\R$ and
$J=M_{1,\tau}$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The valuation formula forces $\tau$ onto. Then $\gap_\tau=0$ by \cref{opa:as:lem:gap}, and an ordered
embedding $\R\to\R$ is the identity; reindexing by $\tau^{-1}$ inverts $M_{1,\tau}$.
\end{proof}

This agrees with \cref{opa:as:thm:factor}: an automorphism of the pair that is exact-monomial is
$M_{1,\tau}$.

\subsection{Taylor sections and the rigidity boundary}\label{opa:as:sub:taylor}

A $\Q$-derivation $d:\R\to\R$ is additive with $d(rs)=r\,d(s)+s\,d(r)$; it need not be continuous.
Given a transcendence basis $B$ of $\R/\Q$, arbitrary values $d(b)$, $b\in B$, extend uniquely to
$\Q(B)$ and then through the algebraic extension $\R/\Q(B)$: if $p(X)=\sum_jc_jX^j$ is the minimal
polynomial of $a$, then $d(a)=-\sum_jd(c_j)a^j/p'(a)$, with $p'(a)\ne0$ in characteristic zero, and
the extensions agree on finite subextensions. So for a transcendental $b$ there is $d$ with $d(b)=1$,
and every such $d$ vanishes on the real algebraic numbers; the choice uses a transcendence basis of
the set $\R/\Q$ and is not computable \src{11, 12, 13}. This is also the derivation used in
\lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow}.

\begin{lemma}[Taylor section \src{11, 12, 13}]\label{opa:as:lem:taylorsection}
For $\delta>0$ in an ordered group and a $\Q$-derivation $d$ of $\R$,
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:taylorsection}
 \rho_{d,\delta}(r)=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac{d^n(r)}{n!}\,t^{n\delta}
\end{equation}
is an ordered field embedding of $\R$ into $\Hahn\R{\Z\delta}$, with constant term $r$; if
$d(b)=1$ then $\rho_{d,\delta}(b)=b+t^\delta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The iterated Leibniz rule $d^n(rs)=\sum_j\binom njd^j(r)d^{n-j}(s)$ gives multiplicativity
coefficientwise; the constant term $r$ gives injectivity and the sign; $d^2(b)=d(1)=0$.
\end{proof}

The ambient version is \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow} of the surcomplex automorphism report, restricted
to real coefficients (a derivation of $\R$ extends to $\C$ with $d(i)=0$, and then the map
preserves $\No$):
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:taylor}
 \mathcal T_{d,\delta}\Bigl(\sum_\lambda r_\lambda t^\lambda\Bigr)
 =\sum_{\lambda,\,n\ge0}\frac{d^n(r_\lambda)}{n!}\,t^{\lambda+n\delta}
\end{equation}
is a strong automorphism of $K_\Delta$ fixing every monomial, preserving valuations and leading
coefficients, with inverse $\mathcal T_{-d,\delta}$ and $v(\mathcal T_{d,\delta}(x)-x)\ge v(x)+\delta$.
11 reproves it and credits the surcomplex report; 12 and 13 use its Taylor ingredient without
that credit. On its own it never preserves $\Oz$ (the remark after \cref{opa:ex:translation}):
with $\delta=1$, $\Delta=\No$ and $d(b)=1$ it sends $\tfrac b2\omega=\tfrac b2t^{-1}$ to
$\tfrac b2\omega+\tfrac12\notin\Oz$ \src{11}. What changes below is its interaction with the image of a
\emph{proper} exponent embedding.

\begin{theorem}[Exact Taylor admissibility \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion}
Let $\delta>0$ and let $d\ne0$ be a derivation of $\R$. The strong exact-monomial embedding
$\mathcal T_{d,\delta}\circ M_{1,\tau}$ preserves $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ if and only if
$\delta\in\gap_\tau$, that is $n\delta<\tau(\gamma)$ for all $\gamma>0$ and $n\ge1$. It is then
$J_{\tau,\rho_{d,\delta}}$ and also reflects $\mathcal R_\Gamma$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
$\mathcal T_{d,\delta}\circ M_{1,\tau}$ is $\sum r_\gamma t^\gamma\mapsto\sum_\gamma\rho_{d,\delta}(r_\gamma)t^{\tau(\gamma)}$. If
$\delta\in\gap_\tau$, then $\rho_{d,\delta}$ takes values in $\Hahn\R{\gap_\tau}$ and
\cref{opa:as:thm:classification} applies. Conversely, for $r$ with $d(r)\ne0$ the coefficient of
$\rho_{d,\delta}(r)$ at $\delta$ is nonzero, and Step 2 of that theorem forces $\delta\in\gap_\tau$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Coinitiality is exactly coefficient rigidity \src{11}]\label{opa:as:cor:rigidity}
For fixed $\tau$, every embedding in \cref{opa:as:thm:classification} fixes $\R$ pointwise if and
only if $\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$ is coinitial in $\Delta^{>0}$. Otherwise some such embedding sends a
chosen transcendental real $b$ to $b+t^\delta$ with $\delta>0$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Coinitial means $\gap_\tau=0$, and an ordered embedding $\R\to\R$ is the identity. Otherwise take
$0<\delta\in\gap_\tau$ and $d(b)=1$ in \cref{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion}.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[A set-sized model \src{11}]\label{opa:as:ex:setmodel}
Let $\Gamma=\Q$, $\Delta=\Q\oplus\Q$ ordered lexicographically with the first coordinate dominant,
and $\tau(q)=(q,0)$. Then $\gap_\tau=\{0\}\oplus\Q$ and $\delta=(0,1)$ is admissible; for $q>0$,
$\rho_{d,\delta}(r)t^{(-q,0)}=\sum_n\frac{d^n(r)}{n!}t^{(-q,n)}$ has only negative exponents. No proper
class is involved.
\end{example}

\section{Explicit proper embeddings of \texorpdfstring{$(\No,\Oz)$}{(No, Oz)}}\label{opa:as:sec:explicit}

\subsection{Two levels of normal-form substitution}

At the \emph{inner} level write an exponent $g\in\No$ in its large-$\omega$ Conway normal form
$g=\sum_{a\in S}r_a\omega^a$, $S$ reverse well ordered. For an order embedding
$\mu:\No\hookrightarrow\No$ put
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:innerlift}
 \iota_\mu(g)=\sum_{a\in S}r_a\,\omega^{\mu(a)} .
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Inner lift \src{11}]\label{opa:as:lem:inner}
$\iota_\mu$ is an $\R$-linear ordered embedding of $(\No,+)$ into itself, with the same leading
coefficient; its image is the class of normal forms supported in $\mu(\No)$, and
$\iota_\mu\iota_{\mu'}=\iota_{\mu\mu'}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
An order embedding preserves reverse well-ordering and identifies no exponents; addition is
coefficientwise. Reindex a normal form supported in $\mu(\No)$ by $\mu^{-1}$ for the image.
\end{proof}

At the \emph{outer} level $\iota_\mu$ is an exponent embedding, and $M_{1,\iota_\mu}$ is a strong
exact-monomial field embedding fixing $\R$ (\cref{opa:as:thm:classification} with $\rho=\id$).
$\iota_\mu$ is only additive: it is not a field map, and $\mu$ need not be additive. The three
sources use three order isomorphisms:
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:mus}
 \mu_{11}(a)=\begin{cases}(1-a)^{-1},&a<0,\\ a+1,&a\ge0,\end{cases}\qquad
 \mu_{13}(a)=a+\sqrt{a^2+1},\qquad
 \mu_{12}(a)=1+a+\sqrt{a^2+1}.
\end{equation}
$\mu_{11}$ maps $\No$ onto $\No^{>0}$, its branches covering $(0,1)$ and $[1,\infty)$. $\mu_{13}$ maps
$\No$ onto $\No^{>0}$ with inverse $y\mapsto(y-y^{-1})/2$, since
$(\sqrt{a^2+1}+a)(\sqrt{a^2+1}-a)=1$ and $y\mapsto(y-y^{-1})/2$ is strictly increasing. So $\mu_{12}=1+\mu_{13}$
maps $\No$ onto $(1,\infty)$. They use only the ordered-field operations of $\No$ \src{11, 12, 13}.

\begin{lemma}[The three value images and their gaps]\label{opa:as:lem:gaps}
The images $\iota_{\mu_{11}}(\No)=\iota_{\mu_{13}}(\No)$ are the additive group of purely infinite
surreals, the normal forms with all exponents positive; $\iota_{\mu_{12}}(\No)$ consists of the normal
forms with all exponents greater than $1$. The gaps are
\[
 \gap_{\iota_{\mu_{11}}}=\gap_{\iota_{\mu_{13}}}=C_1\ \src{11, 13},\qquad
 \gap_{\iota_{\mu_{12}}}=C_\omega\ \mergetag,
\]
the finite surreals and the surreals bounded by an ordinary multiple of $\omega$. All three images are
cofinal in $\No$ and none of their positive parts is coinitial.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The images follow from \cref{opa:as:lem:inner}. A positive purely infinite surreal exceeds every real,
hence every ordinary multiple of a finite surreal; so $C_1\subseteq\gap$. An infinite $c$ has leading
exponent $a>0$, and $\omega^{a/2}$ is a positive image element below $|c|$; so $c\notin\gap$. For
$\mu_{12}$: a positive image element has leading term $r\omega^a$ with $r>0$, $a>1$, so it exceeds
$n\omega$ for every $n$, and $C_\omega\subseteq\gap$. If $c\notin C_\omega$, its leading exponent $a$
exceeds $1$, and $\omega^{(1+a)/2}$ is an image element below $|c|$. Arbitrarily large exponents give
cofinality; no positive image element is at most $1$.
\end{proof}

The purely infinite surreals here form a subgroup of the \emph{value group}; it is the same set as
the ideal $\Pi$ of $\No$, but it is used additively and is not a convex subgroup \src{11, 13}.

\subsection{One map, three instances}

\begin{theorem}[Proper embeddings moving a real \src{11, 12, 13}]\label{opa:as:thm:explicit}
Let $b$ be a transcendental real, $d$ a derivation of $\R$ with $d(b)=1$, and
$\mu\in\{\mu_{11},\mu_{12},\mu_{13}\}$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{opa:as:eq:explicit}
 J_{\mu,d}\Bigl(\sum_\gamma r_\gamma t^\gamma\Bigr)=\sum_\gamma\sum_{n\ge0}\frac{d^n(r_\gamma)}{n!}\,
 t^{\iota_\mu(\gamma)+n}
\end{equation}
is $J_{\iota_\mu,\rho_{d,1}}=\mathcal T_{d,1}\circ M_{1,\iota_\mu}$. It is a proper, strongly additive,
exact-monomial ordered field embedding $\No\hookrightarrow\No$ with
\[
 v(J_{\mu,d}x)=\iota_\mu(v(x)),\quad \ct\circ J_{\mu,d}=\ct,\quad J_{\mu,d}^{-1}(\Oz)=\Oz,\quad
 J_{\mu,d}(b)=b+\omega^{-1},
\]
and it fixes every real algebraic number. Moreover $J_{\mu,d}(\omega)=\omega^\omega$ for
$\mu=\mu_{11},\mu_{13}$ and $J_{\mu_{12},d}(\omega)=\omega^{\omega^2}$. Its restriction is a proper ring
embedding $\Oz\hookrightarrow\Oz$; it is not $\R$-linear, and no automorphism of $(\No,\Oz)$ agrees with
it on $b$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
$1\in\gap_{\iota_\mu}$ by \cref{opa:as:lem:gaps}, so \cref{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion,opa:as:thm:classification}
give the embedding, strongness, valuation, constant term and reflection. $\rho_{d,1}(b)=b+t$ and
$t=\omega^{-1}$; $d$ kills the real algebraic numbers. $\iota_\mu(1)=\omega^{\mu(0)}$ with $\mu_{11}(0)=\mu_{13}(0)=1$
and $\mu_{12}(0)=2$, and $J(\omega)=J(t^{-1})=t^{-\iota_\mu(1)}$. The value $1=v(t)$ is not in
$\iota_\mu(\No)$, so $t\notin J(\No)$; if the restriction to $\Oz$ were onto, extension to fractions
(\lbl{odg:thm:fractions}) would make $J$ onto. Every $\Oz$-preserving automorphism fixes $b$
(\cref{opa:cor:allautos}), and the restriction to $\Oz$ determines the fraction-field map.
\end{proof}

So the rigidity of automorphisms does not pass to embeddings: $(\No,+,\cdot,\Oz)$ and $\Oz$ have
proper self-embeddings, and an ordered-field embedding of $\No$ preserving and reflecting $\Oz$ need
not preserve $\R$ setwise, even when it is strongly additive and preserves constant terms exactly
\src{12}. 11 proves the theorem for $\mu_{11}$ through its classification; 12 (for $\mu_{12}$) and 13
(for $\mu_{13}$) give the same block argument directly. The three maps differ only in the order isomorphism: 11's and
13's have the same value image and send $\omega\mapsto\omega^\omega$. For $\mu_{12}$, $\gap=C_\omega$, so even
$\delta=\omega$ would be admissible (\cref{opa:as:lem:gaps}). For example
\[
 J_{\mu,d}(bt^g)=bt^{\iota_\mu(g)}+t^{\iota_\mu(g)+1}\ (g<0),\qquad
 J_{\mu_{11},d}(b\omega)=b\omega^\omega+\omega^{\omega-1}\in\Oz,
\]
both terms purely infinite although $J(b)$ is not real; and
$J(b^2)=b^2+2bt+t^2$, $J(b^{-1})=\sum_n(-1)^nb^{-n-1}t^n$, $J(t^g)=t^{\iota_\mu(g)}$ \src{11, 12}.
The ordinary Taylor section alone is not a map on arbitrary normal forms, since supports could meet
its integer shifts; the proper exponent embedding separates the scales \src{12, 13}.

\begin{corollary}[Image, reals and a missing test \src{12}; for all three \mergetag]\label{opa:as:cor:image}
For each $J=J_{\mu,d}$: $J(\No)\cap\R=\ker d$, and $\ct(tJ(x))=0$ for every $x\in\No$, although
$t\ne0$. So the image does not supply all constant-term tests.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
If $J(x)$ is constant, every block at $g\ne0$ vanishes, so $x=r$ is real, and $\rho_{d,1}(r)$ is constant
exactly when $d(r)=0$ (see also \cref{opa:as:prop:overlap}). An exponent $\iota_\mu(g)+n=-1$ would make
$\iota_\mu(g)$ a negative integer; but $\iota_\mu(g)$ is $0$ or of absolute value above every integer
(\cref{opa:as:lem:gaps}). So no image has a term at $-1$.
\end{proof}

12 proves the corollary for $\mu_{12}$; the merge notes that its proof uses only that nonzero values of
$\iota_\mu$ are infinite, true for all three. The annihilator is the missing detector in the isomorphism
proof: tests from the image cannot distinguish $t$ from $0$, whereas the ambient test $t^{-1}$ does
\src{12}.

\begin{corollary}[Failure of pair homogeneity \src{13}]\label{opa:as:cor:homogeneity}
The ordered embedding $\rho_{d,1}:\R\to\No$ is an embedding of the induced pairs $(\R,\Z)\to(\No,\Oz)$
that extends to the proper pair embedding $J_{\mu,d}$ but to no automorphism of $(\No,\Oz)$. So
$(\No,\Oz)$ is not strongly homogeneous for embeddings of set-sized induced substructures.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Restrict the reflection property of \cref{opa:as:thm:explicit} to $\R$; every $\Oz$-preserving automorphism
fixes $b$, whereas $\rho_{d,1}(b)=b+t$.
\end{proof}

``Strongly homogeneous'' means extension of isomorphisms between set-sized induced substructures, not
extension of elementary partial maps in a saturated expansion. The pure ordered field $\No$ has this
extension property for set-sized ordered subfields \cite[Remark 2.10]{KKS}, so $\rho_{d,1}$ does extend to
an ordered-field automorphism of $\No$, and every such extension fails to preserve $\Oz$; the obstruction
lies in the integer part \src{13}. The map also satisfies $\ct\circ J=\ct$ but not $J\circ\ct=\ct$ nor
$J\circ\ct=\ct\circ J$: for $b$ these compare $b$ with $b+t$. It violates no hypothesis of
\cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso}, since it is not onto \src{13}. It does not commute with the omega-map:
$J(\omega^1)=\omega^\omega$ for $\mu_{11}$ whereas $\omega^{J(1)}=\omega$; no question of \cite{KKS} on the
omega-map is addressed \src{11}.

\section{Closed images and the two ends of the value group}\label{opa:as:sec:topology}

Use the valuation topology with basic neighbourhoods $\mathcal V_\lambda=\{x:v(x)>\lambda\}$. On a real Hahn
field with nonzero value group it is the order topology; for $\No$ it is the fine topology. No countable
basis is assumed, and for proper classes the topological statements are local conditions on balls; no
object containing all open subclasses is formed \src{11}.

\begin{theorem}[Closed images \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:closed}
Every image $F=J_{\tau,\rho}(K_\Gamma)$ of \cref{opa:as:thm:classification} is closed in $K_\Delta$; if $J$ is
proper, $F$ is nowhere dense.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Put $\mathsf G=\gap_\tau$ and $D=\tau(\Gamma)+\mathsf G$; image series are supported in $D$. If $x$ has a
nonzero coefficient at $\lambda\notin D$, then $v(x-y)\le\lambda$ for all $y\in F$. If $\supp x\subseteq D$, split
$x=\sum_{h\in S}t^hx_h$ with $x_h\in\Hahn\R{\mathsf G}$ by the unique decomposition; the projection
$D\to\tau(\Gamma)$ is nondecreasing, so $S$ is well ordered. Put $r_h=\ct(x_h)$. If $x_h=\rho(r_h)$ for all
$h$, then $x=J_{\tau,\rho}(\sum_hr_ht^{\tau^{-1}(h)})\in F$. Otherwise pick $h$ and $c\in\mathsf G$ where
$x_h-\rho(r_h)$ has a nonzero coefficient, and put $L=\max\{h,h+c\}$. The block of any $y\in F$ at $h$ is
$\rho(s)$ for one $s\in\R$ (possibly $0$); if $s\ne r_h$ then $x-y$ has a nonzero coefficient at $h$, and if
$s=r_h$ at $h+c$. So $x+\mathcal V_L$ misses $F$, and $F$ is closed. A subfield containing a ball $\mathcal V_L$
contains every $z\ne0$, as $z=(zt^\gamma)/t^\gamma$ with both in $\mathcal V_L$ for large $\gamma$; so a proper
subfield has empty interior, and a closed one is nowhere dense.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Induced value embedding \src{11}]\label{opa:as:lem:natural}
Every field embedding $f:\No\hookrightarrow\No$ is ordered and induces an ordered additive embedding
$\tau_f$ of the natural value group with $v(f(x))=\tau_f(v(x))$. No monomial or strongness hypothesis is
needed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Positivity is being a nonzero square. Archimedean equivalence and strict domination are expressed by
inequalities with ordinary integers, which $f$ preserves and reflects; multiplicativity gives additivity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Continuity or external discreteness \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:topology}
Let $f:\No\hookrightarrow\No$ be any field embedding. Exactly one of the following holds.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},itemsep=1pt]
\item $\tau_f(\No)$ is cofinal in $\No$. Then $f$ is continuous, a homeomorphism onto its image, and the
image is nondiscrete.
\item $\tau_f(\No)$ is not cofinal. Then there is $L$ with $v(y-z)<L$ for all distinct $y,z\in f(\No)$; the
image is closed and uniformly discrete, and $f$ is nowhere continuous.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(i) Given $L$, choose $\gamma$ with $\tau_f(\gamma)>L$; then $f(\mathcal V_\gamma)=f(\No)\cap\mathcal V_{\tau_f(\gamma)}
\subseteq\mathcal V_L$. (ii) Take $L$ above $\tau_f(\No)$: nonzero differences in the image have value below
$L$, so $f^{-1}(\mathcal V_L)=\{0\}$, not open, and additivity gives discontinuity everywhere. A ball of
radius $L$ contains at most one image point, which gives closedness and uniform discreteness.
\end{proof}

There is no contradiction in an order-isomorphic copy of the nondiscrete $\No$ being discrete: the intrinsic
order topology of a non-cofinal ordered subfield can be coarser than its subspace topology; the inverse
from the image stays continuous \src{11}.

\begin{theorem}[The two ends are independent \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:four}
For the order embeddings
\[
 \mu^{++}(a)=\begin{cases}a,&a\le0,\\a+1,&a>0,\end{cases}\qquad
 \mu^{+-}=\mu_{11},
\]
\[
 \mu^{-+}(a)=-\mu_{11}(-a),\qquad
 \mu^{--}(a)=\frac{\mu_{11}(a)}{1+\mu_{11}(a)},
\]
with ranges $(-\infty,0]\cup(1,\infty)$, $(0,\infty)$, $(-\infty,0)$ and $(0,1)$, the value group
$\iota_\mu(\No)$ is respectively cofinal and coinitial, cofinal and not coinitial, coinitial and not
cofinal, neither; here ``coinitial'' refers to the positive part. In each case there is a proper strong
exact-monomial self-embedding preserving and reflecting $\Oz$: $M_{1,\iota_\mu}$ in the coinitial cases,
where coefficient motion is impossible, and $\mathcal T_{d,1}\circ M_{1,\iota_\mu}$, moving $b$ to
$b+\omega^{-1}$, in the others. The embeddings are continuous with closed nondiscrete image in the cofinal
cases and nowhere continuous with discrete image in the others; all four images are nowhere dense.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By \cref{opa:as:lem:inner} the value image is the class of normal forms supported in the range. The range of
$\mu^{++}$ has arbitrarily high and low levels and misses $(0,1]$, so the image is proper, cofinal and
coinitial. $\mu^{+-}$ is \cref{opa:as:lem:gaps}. For $\mu^{-+}$ all values are infinitesimal, so the group is
bounded by $1$, but monomials at arbitrarily negative levels make it coinitial. For $\mu^{--}$ all nonzero
values are purely infinite and below $\omega$ in absolute value, so the group is neither; its gap is $C_1$,
since a finite value is dominated by every positive image value and an infinite value with leading level
$a>0$ dominates $\omega^c$ for some $0<c<\min\{a,1\}$. Apply \cref{opa:as:cor:rigidity,opa:as:thm:closed,opa:as:thm:topology}.
\end{proof}

So cofinality must not replace coinitiality in the rigidity criterion: $J_{\mu_{11},d}$ is continuous with
cofinal values and still moves $\R$ \src{11}.

\section{Constants, elementarity, conjugacy and parameters}\label{opa:as:sec:model}

\subsection{Elementary reduct, nonelementary expansion}

$\R$ is parameter-free definable in $(K_\Gamma,\mathcal R_\Gamma)$ by the existential test, the multiplier
condition and its units (\lbl{odg:def:thm:ideal}, \lbl{odg:def:thm:multiplier}; the internal form is
\lbl{odg:def:cor:internal}); 11 reproves this with explicit formulas $D$, $M$, $\mathrm{Const}$, and derives
the rigidity of automorphisms (\cref{opa:cor:allautos}).

\begin{theorem}[Logical separation \src{11}; \src{12} for its example]\label{opa:as:thm:elementary}
Every field embedding $\No\hookrightarrow\No$ is elementary as an embedding of ordered fields. An embedding
of \cref{opa:as:thm:classification} (with $\Gamma=\Delta=\No$) that moves some real is not elementary as an
embedding of $(\No,\Oz)$, although it preserves and reflects $\Oz$, and its restriction to $\Oz$ is not
elementary as an embedding of rings.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The first assertion is quantifier elimination for real closed ordered fields \cite{Marker}, read formula by
formula for the class structure. If $\Phi(b)\notin\R$, the formula $\mathrm{Const}(x)$ defining $\R$ holds of
$b$ and fails of $\Phi(b)$. For $\Phi(b)=b+t$ the new target witness is visible: $\omega\in\Pi$ but
$(b+t)\omega=b\omega+1\notin\Pi$; the universal multiplier condition quantifies over more elements in the
target. For the ring: $\Frac\Oz=\No$ (\lbl{odg:thm:fractions}), the fraction field is uniformly interpretable
in the ring by pairs modulo $ad=bc$, with the ring as the class of fractions $(z,1)$, so an elementary ring
embedding would induce an elementary embedding of the interpreted pair; represent $b$ as $(b\omega)/\omega$.
\end{proof}

The first assertion is classical. The new point is that a strong, exact-monomial, predicate-reflecting
embedding can lose elementarity in the omnific expansion; coefficient fixing is not proved sufficient for
elementarity (\cref{opa:as:q:elementary}) \src{11}. 12 records the same failure: the multiplier definition of
$\Ak$ quantifies over all of $\Pi$, which an embedding need not preserve. This sharpens the Diophantine
report's caution after \lbl{odg:def:thm:autreal} that its argument does not apply unchanged to endomorphisms:
there are proper ring embeddings $\Oz\hookrightarrow\Oz$ whose fraction-field extensions move a real.

\begin{proposition}[Ordinary overlap \src{11}; \src{12} for Taylor sections]\label{opa:as:prop:overlap}
For $F=J_{\tau,\rho}(K_\Gamma)$: $F\cap\Hahn\R{\gap_\tau}=\rho(\R)$ and $F\cap\R=\{r:\rho(r)=r\}$, a real
closed subfield of $\R$; for $\rho=\rho_{d,\delta}$ it is $\ker d$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
A nonzero block at $h\ne0$ lies in $h+\gap_\tau$, disjoint from $\gap_\tau$, so an image in
$\Hahn\R{\gap_\tau}$ is its zero block $\rho(r)$; if that is a real constant, its standard part gives $r$. If
$r$ is algebraic over the fixed field $k$, then $\rho(r)$ is a root of the same polynomial, all real roots lie in
$\R$, and the residue singles out $r$; so $k$ is relatively algebraically closed in the real closed $\R$, hence
real closed. $\rho_{d,\delta}(r)=r$ exactly when $d(r)=0$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Continuum many copies separated by the omnific integers}

\begin{lemma}[One-sided infinitesimal neighbourhoods \src{11}]\label{opa:as:lem:side}
Let $F=J_{\tau,\rho}(K_\Gamma)$ and $\rho(r)\ne r$. Every $y\in F$ with $\st(y)=r$ has
$\operatorname{sgn}(y-r)=\operatorname{sgn}(\rho(r)-r)$, and $\rho(r)$ is such a $y$. For $\rho=\rho_{d,\delta}$
with $d(r)\ne0$ the sign is that of $d(r)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$y=J(x)$ with $x=r+\varepsilon$, $v(\varepsilon)>0$ (by the valuation and constant-term formulas). The nonzero
$\rho(r)-r$ has value in $\gap_\tau^{>0}$, while $v(J\varepsilon)\in\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$ is larger; so
$y-r=(\rho(r)-r)+J(\varepsilon)$ has the leading term of $\rho(r)-r$, which is $d(r)t^\delta$ for a Taylor section.
\end{proof}

The sign is invariant under every field automorphism of $\No$ fixing $\R$ pointwise, in particular under every
$\Oz$-preserving one (\cref{opa:cor:allautos}), since these preserve order and standard part.

\begin{theorem}[One field-conjugacy class, continuum many pair classes \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:continuum}
There are proper strong exact-monomial embeddings $J_a:\No\hookrightarrow\No$, $a\in\R$, with images $F_a$, such
that
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},itemsep=1pt]
\item $J_a^{-1}(\Oz)=\Oz$;
\item all $F_a$ have the cofinal value image $\iota_{\mu_{11}}(\No)$ and are closed, nondiscrete and nowhere dense;
\item all $F_a$ are conjugate under strong valuation-fixing field automorphisms of $\No$;
\item for $a\ne a'$ no field automorphism of $\No$ fixing $\R$ pointwise, in particular none preserving $\Oz$,
maps $F_a$ onto $F_{a'}$.
\end{enumerate}
So the pairs $(F_a,F_a\cap\Oz)$ are all isomorphic to $(\No,\Oz)$ but lie in pairwise inequivalent positions.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Choose distinct $b_1,b_2$ in a transcendence basis of $\R/\Q$ and let $d_a$ be the derivation with $d_a(b_1)=1$,
$d_a(b_2)=a$ and $d_a=0$ on the other basis elements; no independence of named constants is assumed. Put
$J_a=\mathcal T_{d_a,1}\circ M_{1,\iota_{\mu_{11}}}$. (i) and (ii) are \cref{opa:as:thm:explicit,opa:as:thm:closed,opa:as:thm:topology}.
For (iii), $\mathcal T_{d_{a'},1}\mathcal T_{d_a,1}^{-1}$ maps $F_a$ onto $F_{a'}$; the derivations need not commute,
and only the individual inverses are used. For (iv) let $a<q<a'$ with $q\in\Q$ and $r=b_2-qb_1$: $d_a(r)=a-q<0<a'-q
=d_{a'}(r)$, so by \cref{opa:as:lem:side} the points of $F_a$ with standard part $r$ lie below $r$ and those of $F_{a'}$
above, and both sets are nonempty. Finally $F_a\cap\Oz=J_a(\Oz)$ by reflection.
\end{proof}

This is a lower bound of continuum on the pair-conjugacy types inside one field-conjugacy class, not a
classification or an upper bound; the images have identical value data, so no classification can use the value
image and its cuts alone \src{11}. It partly answers 13's question on the conjugacy of its drift images
(\cref{opa:as:q:conjugacy}).

\subsection{Proper copies fixing prescribed parameters}

\begin{lemma}[Two-level support control \src{11}]\label{opa:as:lem:parameters}
For a set $A\subseteq\No$ let $B$ be the union of the inner supports of the outer exponents of the members of $A$,
a set. Every order embedding $\mu$ fixing $B$ pointwise has $M_{1,\iota_\mu}(a)=a$ for all $a\in A$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$\iota_\mu$ fixes every outer exponent of every member of $A$; this is the inner/outer distinction of
\cref{opa:lem:avoid}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Parameter-fixed discrete and nondiscrete copies \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:parameters}
For every set $A\subseteq\No$ there are proper strong exact-monomial embeddings $f_A,g_A:\No\hookrightarrow\No$
fixing $A\cup\R$ pointwise with $f_A^{-1}(\Oz)=g_A^{-1}(\Oz)=\Oz$. The image of $f_A$ is closed and uniformly
discrete and $f_A$ is nowhere continuous; the image of $g_A$ is closed, nondiscrete and nowhere dense and $g_A$ is a
homeomorphism onto it; no field automorphism of $\No$ maps one image onto the other. There is an explicit
$\Omega>1$ with $\Omega^{-1}<|y|<\Omega$ for every $0\ne y\in f_A(\No)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take $B$ as in \cref{opa:as:lem:parameters} and $u$ above $B$. The map $\mu^{\mathrm{cmp}}(x)=x$ for $x\le u$,
$u+\frac{x-u}{1+x-u}$ for $x>u$, is an order isomorphism onto $(-\infty,u+1)$ fixing $B$, so every value $h$ of
$\iota_{\mu^{\mathrm{cmp}}}$ has $|h|<\beta=\omega^{u+1}$; put $f_A=M_{1,\iota_{\mu^{\mathrm{cmp}}}}$. The map
$\mu^{\mathrm{jmp}}(x)=x$ for $x\le u$, $x+1$ for $x>u$, fixes $B$, omits $(u,u+1]$ and has cofinal range; put
$g_A=M_{1,\iota_{\mu^{\mathrm{jmp}}}}$. \Cref{opa:as:thm:classification,opa:as:lem:parameters,opa:as:thm:closed,opa:as:thm:topology}
give the properties. A field automorphism of $\No$ is a homeomorphism (its value map is onto;
\cref{opa:as:lem:natural,opa:as:thm:topology}) and cannot exchange a discrete and a nondiscrete subspace. For
$\Omega=\omega^\beta$: a nonzero $y\in f_A(\No)$ has natural value $h$ with $|h|<\beta$, so its leading monomial has
exponent below $\beta$ and $|y|<\Omega$; apply this to $1/y$.
\end{proof}

The annulus bounds are external: $\Omega$ is not in the image, which is internally isomorphic to $\No$ and not
Archimedean \src{11}.

\emph{Extension elsewhere} (batch 31). The independent-copies report \fn{docs/surreal/independent-surreal-copies}
extends the fixing clause, with $\rho=\id$, from two maps to an $\On$-indexed family with a prescribed intersection
(its \lbl{isc:cor:parameters}, from \lbl{isc:main:copies}). Let $H$ be the $\Q$-span of the supports of the members of
$A$. There are proper strong exact-monomial embeddings $\Phi_\alpha$ ($\alpha\in\On$) preserving and reflecting $\Oz$
and fixing the full Hahn field $\Hahn\R H\supseteq A\cup\R$ pointwise; any two images meet exactly in $\Hahn\R H$, and
every finite subfamily is jointly linearly disjoint over it. That field is the full support closure of $A$, not the
field generated by $A$ or its real closure. In the terms of \cref{opa:as:thm:classification} these maps are
$J_{h_\alpha,\id}$ for exponent embeddings $h_\alpha$ fixing $H$ with ranges independent over $H$
(\lbl{isc:thm:group}), obtained by class bookkeeping. For $H=0$ that report also gives explicit maps
(\lbl{isc:thm:explicit}): $M_{1,\iota_{\sigma_\alpha}}$ with $\sigma_\alpha$ an order isomorphism of $\No$ onto the
interval $(3\alpha,3\alpha+1)$. These intervals are bounded and pairwise disjoint, and the bottom gap is nonzero. That
report makes no topological assertion. \mergetag: the exponent map of each explicit copy has bounded range, so by
\cref{opa:as:thm:topology}(ii) its image is closed and uniformly discrete, as for $f_A$; for the general family the
alternative is not decided.

\begin{theorem}[Relative coefficient motion \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:relative}
Let $A\subseteq\No$ be a set and $k_A$ the field generated over $\Q$ by the real coefficients of the members of $A$.
If $b\in\R$ is transcendental over $k_A$, there is a proper strong exact-monomial embedding fixing $A$ pointwise,
preserving and reflecting $\Oz$, with cofinal value image and $b\mapsto b+t^\delta$ for some $\delta>0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Choose $d$ zero on $k_A$ with $d(b)=1$, through a transcendence basis of $\R/k_A$ containing $b$. With $B$ as in
\cref{opa:as:lem:parameters}, choose $v$ below $B$ and $c<v$, and let $\mu^{\mathrm{rel}}(x)=v-(v-c)\frac{v-x}{1+v-x}$
for $x<v$ and $x$ for $x\ge v$, an order isomorphism onto $(c,\infty)$ fixing $B$. Positive values of
$\iota_{\mu^{\mathrm{rel}}}$ have leading exponent above $c$, so $\delta=\omega^c$ lies in the gap. Put
$\mathcal T_{d,\delta}\circ M_{1,\iota_{\mu^{\mathrm{rel}}}}$; it fixes $A$ since the exponent lift fixes each member
and $d$ kills its coefficients. Apply \cref{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion}.
\end{proof}

An ordered embedding fixing a real field fixes the reals algebraic over it, so one cannot fix every real and move
another; the transcendence hypothesis is needed \src{11}.

\section{Surcomplex and Gaussian versions}\label{opa:as:sec:complex}

Identify $\SC=\Hahn\C\No$ coefficientwise with conjugation $\overline{x+iy}=x-iy$; the union of the real and
imaginary supports is well ordered, and a family of surcomplex series is summable if and only if its real and
imaginary families are. The general Gaussian problem is kept separate from two restricted ones: with conjugation
named, and with the natural valuation assumed preserved. They must not be merged into an unconditional
classification \src{12, 13}.

\begin{theorem}[Conjugation-compatible automorphisms \src{12, 13, 14}]\label{opa:as:thm:conjugation}
Every field automorphism $F$ of $\SC$ with $F(\Og)=\Og$ that commutes with conjugation has the form
$F(x+iy)=\sigma(x)+\varepsilon i\sigma(y)$ with $\sigma$ an $\Oz$-preserving automorphism of $\No$ and
$\varepsilon=\pm1$, and $F$ and $F^{-1}$ are strongly additive. Conversely every such map has these properties, so
this stabilizer is $\Aut(\Oz)\times C_2$; with $F(i)=i$ imposed, only $\varepsilon=1$ occurs and $F$ is strongly
$\C$-linear.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Commuting with conjugation preserves its fixed field $\No$, and $\Og\cap\No=\Oz$. $F(i)$ is a root of $X^2+1$.
\Cref{opa:as:thm:main} makes $\sigma$ strong, and summability is coordinatewise. Real lifts commute with
conjugation, which makes the product direct.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Valuation-compatible Gaussian automorphisms \src{12, 13, 14}]\label{opa:as:thm:gaussian}
Let $F$ be a field automorphism of $\SC$ with $F(\Og)=\Og$ that maps the Hahn valuation ring onto itself
(equivalently $v(Fx)=\tau(v(x))$ for an ordered automorphism $\tau$ of $\No$; values need not be fixed). Then $F(\C)=\C$;
with $\alpha=F|_\C$, $F$ is $\alpha$-semilinear, $\ct(Fx)=\alpha(\ct x)$, and $F$ and $F^{-1}$ are strongly additive. The
same holds for the fraction-field extension of a ring automorphism of $\Og$ whenever that extension preserves the
valuation, and for valuation-compatible isomorphisms between full complex Hahn fields with nonzero exponent groups
preserving their Gaussian integer parts \src{13}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
$\Pi_\C=\bigcap_nn\Og$ (\cref{opa:lem:divisible}); its multiplier ring is $\C\oplus\Pi_\C$ with units $\C^\times$
(\lbl{odg:def:thm:multiplier}), so $F(\C)=\C$. Valuation compatibility preserves $\mm_\C$, and
$\SC=\Pi_\C\oplus\C\oplus\mm_\C$ gives $\ct(Fx)=\alpha(\ct x)$. Apply \cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso}. The fraction-field
statement uses $\Frac\Og=\SC$ (\lbl{odg:thm:fractions}); the isomorphism statement uses only the ambient fields.
\end{proof}

$\alpha$ may be a wild automorphism of $\C$: coefficientwise application of any automorphism of $\C$ is strong and
preserves $\Og$, since it fixes $\Q$ and sends $i$ to $\pm i$ \src{13}. This is consistent with the wild lift of
\cref{opa:prop:wild} and with \lbl{odg:def:prop:coeffaut}, which are strong. Neither theorem shows that every
automorphism of $\Og$ has a valuation-compatible extension; conjugation is not assumed intrinsic, and maps moving
the real axis are already in the collection (\cref{opa:thm:realaxis}, \lbl{odg:def:lem:twist}). Since
$\Frac\Og=\SC$, the open issue is compatibility, not existence of the extension \src{12, 13}
(\cref{opa:as:q:gaussian}).

\begin{theorem}[Conjugation-compatible embeddings \src{11}]\label{opa:as:thm:complexembed}
The strong field embeddings $\Psi:K^\C_\Gamma\hookrightarrow K^\C_\Delta$ that commute with conjugation, satisfy
$\Psi(t^\gamma)=t^{\tau(\gamma)}$ and preserve $\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma$ are exactly
$\Psi(x+iy)=J_{\tau,\rho}(x)+\varepsilon iJ_{\tau,\rho}(y)$, $\varepsilon=\pm1$, with $(\tau,\rho)$ as in
\cref{opa:as:thm:classification}; each satisfies $\Psi^{-1}(\mathcal R^\C_\Delta)=\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma$, and $\Psi(i)=i$
forces $\varepsilon=1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Commuting with conjugation maps $K_\Gamma$ into $K_\Delta$, and $\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma\cap K_\Gamma=\mathcal R_\Gamma$, so
the restriction is classified; $\Psi(i)=\pm i$. The converse is coordinatewise, and $\mathcal R^\C$-membership is
membership of both coordinates.
\end{proof}

12's and 13's surcomplex embeddings $x+iy\mapsto J(x)+iJ(y)$ are the case $\varepsilon=1$ of
\cref{opa:as:thm:explicit}; they are proper and strong, commute with conjugation, reflect $\Og$, move $b$ to $b+t$,
and their image meets $\C$ in $\ker d+i\ker d$ \src{12, 13}.

\begin{proposition}[Complex constants and nonelementarity \src{11}]\label{opa:as:prop:complexconst}
$\C$ is parameter-free definable in $(K^\C_\Gamma,\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma)$. An embedding of
\cref{opa:as:thm:complexembed} with nontrivial $\rho$ is elementary as an embedding of algebraically closed fields,
but not in the Gaussian expansion; for $\Gamma=\Delta=\No$ its restriction to $\Og$ is not elementary as a ring
embedding.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $z\in\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma$, $z\in\Pi^\C_\Gamma$ if and only if $z^2=2w^2$ for some $w\in\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma$, since
$\sqrt2\notin\Q(i)$; the multiplier ring and its units define $\C$ as in the real case (\lbl{odg:def:cor:internal}). A
moved real $b$ has $\rho(b)\notin\C$. Pure-field elementarity is quantifier elimination for algebraically closed fields
\cite{Marker}; for the ring use $\Frac\Og=\SC$ and the interpreted fraction field as in \cref{opa:as:thm:elementary}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Transferred copies \src{11}]\label{opa:as:cor:complexcopies}
The explicit coefficient-moving embedding, the four topological cases and the parameter-fixed copies extend to $\SC$,
preserving and reflecting $\Og$; parameters in a set $A\subseteq\SC$ are handled through their real and imaginary parts.
The family of \cref{opa:as:thm:continuum} gives field-conjugate surcomplex images that are pairwise nonconjugate under
automorphisms preserving \emph{both} conjugation and $\Og$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use $\varepsilon=1$; the valuation of $x+iy$ is the minimum of the coordinate valuations, so the topology is the
product topology. An automorphism preserving conjugation and $\Og$ restricts to an $\Oz$-preserving automorphism of
$\No$, and each extended image meets $\No$ in the real image; apply \cref{opa:as:thm:continuum}.
\end{proof}

No nonconjugacy is claimed for automorphisms preserving $\Og$ but not conjugation, which need not fix the real axis
pointwise, and non-conjugation-compatible surcomplex embeddings are not classified \src{11}.

\section{Boundaries of the method}\label{opa:as:sec:bound}

\begin{proposition}[Regular cardinal support bounds \src{12}]\label{opa:as:prop:kappa}
Let $\kappa$ be a regular uncountable cardinal, $\Gamma$ a set, and $K_{<\kappa}$ the field of series in $\Hahn k\Gamma$
with support of size $<\kappa$, where summability also requires total support of size $<\kappa$. A family in
$K_{<\kappa}$ is summable there if and only if fewer than $\kappa$ members are nonzero and for every $y\in K_{<\kappa}$
only finitely many $\ct(x_jy)$ are nonzero. Consequently a constant-term-covariant field isomorphism between two such
fields is strong for this restricted summation.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
A summable family has fewer than $\kappa$ nonzero members, since each contributes at an exponent of a total support of
size $<\kappa$ and each exponent receives finitely many; pairing finiteness is \cref{opa:as:thm:detect}. Conversely,
regularity bounds the union of fewer than $\kappa$ supports of size $<\kappa$; monomials test pointwise finiteness, and
the countable witness of \cref{opa:as:lem:triangular} lies in $K_{<\kappa}$ because $\kappa$ is uncountable. An
isomorphism preserves the number of nonzero members, and the proof of \cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso} carries over.
\end{proof}

The cardinal condition cannot be dropped: a strictly increasing $(g_\alpha)_{\alpha<\kappa}$ in $\Gamma$ gives
$(t^{g_\alpha})$ with finite pairings against every $y\in K_{<\kappa}$ (\cref{opa:as:lem:intersection}) but total
support of size $\kappa$ \src{12}. For singular $\kappa$ the union argument fails, and no extension is claimed; the
proposition identifies no birthday cutoff with a support-bounded Hahn field \src{12}. 13 records the regular case only
inside its singular-cardinal question (\cref{opa:as:q:singular}).

\begin{example}[Small integer parts \src{13, 14}]\label{opa:as:ex:smallfrac}
The proof of $\Frac\Oz=\No$ uses an upper bound in $\No$ for every set of exponents, and does not transfer to set-sized
groups. For $\Gamma=\Z$ and $u=t^{-1}$, $\mathcal R_\Z=\Z\oplus u\R[u]$ and $\Frac\mathcal R_\Z=\R(u)\ne\R((t))$:
$ru/u=r$ gives $\R(u)$, and $f=\sum_{n\ge1}t^{n!}$ is not rational, since a rational power series has coefficients
obeying an eventual linear recurrence, and arbitrarily long zero blocks followed by a $1$ contradict one.
\end{example}

This is the phenomenon of \cref{opa:par:prop:fracworkspace} and of \cite[Proposition 2.4.5]{LM}; it is why
\cref{opa:as:cor:setpairs} assumes an ambient isomorphism \src{11, 12, 13}.

\begin{remark}[Distinctions kept \src{12, 13}]\label{opa:as:rem:checklist}
The detector must lie in the field tested: automatic for full Hahn fields and for the countable witness in
$K_{<\kappa}$, not for arbitrary subfields (a sufficient condition is 14's countable detector property,
\cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace}, and \cref{opa:cm:ex:puiseux} is a subfield where detection fails). Strongness is not $\R$-linearity: $J_{\mu,d}$ is strong but not
$\R$-linear, so the adjoint theorem, which is for $k$-linear maps, does not apply to it by treating its coefficient lift
as scalar multiplication. The retraction $\ret_\phi$ is linear, never a field retraction for a proper extension. The
adjoint theorem needs a set of exponents; automatic strongness of isomorphisms does not. No result of this part concerns
the Gonshor exponential, logarithm, the Berarducci--Mantova derivation, the omega-map, birthdays or simplicity; the
square roots in \eqref{opa:as:eq:mus} are ordered-field operations reindexing an additive group. No proper-class Zorn
lemma and no basis of the class $\No$ is used; the only bases chosen are a transcendence basis and a Hamel basis of the
set $\R$ over $\Q$ \src{13}.
\end{remark}

\section{Status of the questions, and questions of this part}\label{opa:as:sec:questions}

\subsection{Answered and re-scoped questions}\label{opa:as:sub:status}

\begin{itemize}[itemsep=2pt]
\item \Cref{opa:q:strong} is \emph{answered}. Every ring automorphism of $\Oz$ extends to a strong
automorphism of $\No$ (\cref{opa:as:thm:main}), so preservation of $\Oz$ forces strongness and there is no
nonstrong $\Oz$-preserving example; 09's characterization of the nonstrong pair automorphisms is vacuous, and
10's question whether every $\Oz$-preserving $1$-automorphism is strong has the answer yes. The analogous
question for $\Og$ has a positive answer under conjugation or valuation compatibility and is open otherwise
(\cref{opa:as:q:gaussian}).
\item \lbl{osq:q:invisible} of \fn{docs/surreal/set-sized-quotients-of-omnific-integers}: its second clause
is \emph{answered} by \cref{opa:as:cor:invisible}; the nonstrong case left open in \cref{opa:sub:status}
does not exist. Its first clause, on class quotient domains, is untouched. The quotient report is not edited
by this merge; since batch 31 it records the question as partly answered, with this corollary.
\item \Cref{opa:q:characters}: unchanged. 12 and 13 ask it again, in the forms ``for which support-controlled
families of logarithmic characters does $a_\delta(C_\delta)=0$ imply two-sided substitution admissibility?''
and ``give necessary and sufficient support conditions on additive $\gamma\mapsto L_\gamma\in\mm$ for
$t^\gamma\mapsto t^\gamma\exp L_\gamma$ to extend to a strong automorphism, assuming $a_\delta(C_\delta)=0$'',
suggesting common-shift and finite-rank triangular classes. Automatic strongness settles the recognition of
existing automorphisms, not their construction.
\item \Cref{opa:q:generation} now concerns every automorphism of $(\No,\Oz)$ with trivial monomial factor,
not only the strong ones; it is otherwise unchanged.
\item \Cref{opa:sc:q:otherparts} is related to 12's question on more intrinsic scalar tests, merged into
\cref{opa:as:q:otherrings}.
\item Scope of the answers: $\Oz$, the full real Hahn pairs, and $\Og$ with conjugation or valuation data.
For general characteristic-zero $k$ and unital $\oo$ the non-claims of 04, 09 and 10 that strongness is not
shown (\cref{opa:app:nonclaims}) still apply (\cref{opa:as:q:otherrings}).
\end{itemize}

The novelty statements of 11, 12 and 13 were checked against the tree at the merge. No report proves automatic
strongness, the scalar detection of summability, the adjoint theorems, or coefficient-moving embeddings of
$(\No,\Oz)$; their statements about this report and the Diophantine report were accurate at their pins. What
they reprove (reconstruction, the criterion, the strong factorization, the Taylor automorphism) is credited
above. Priority remains provisional, as each source says. Source 14's novelty statement (batch 31), accurate at
its pin but stale at the merge, is recorded in \cref{opa:cm:sub:stale}.

\subsection{Questions}\label{opa:as:sub:questions}

Each question is a proposed research direction, not a published open problem \src{11, 12, 13}. Duplicates across
the three sources are merged, with every source named.

\begin{question}[The unrestricted Gaussian stabilizer \src{12, 13, 14}]\label{opa:as:q:gaussian}
Does every ring automorphism of $\Og$ have a valuation-compatible extension to $\SC$, that is, is $\mm_\C$
invariant? Can the positive-support component be recovered from $\Og$ and its fraction field without naming
conjugation or a valuation?
\end{question}
$\C$ and $\Pi_\C$ are already intrinsic, so the missing object is $\mm_\C$; a positive answer with
\cref{opa:as:thm:gaussian} would give automatic strongness for all automorphisms of $\Og$. A negative answer
should exhibit an automorphism of the ring violating a valuation inequality, not merely moving the real axis or
being an unrelated automorphism of the algebraically closed fraction field \src{12, 13}. 14 asks the same, adding
whether equivariance of the full constant term at least follows, and proposes first targets: the full field
$\Hahn\C\Q$, then a rank-two lexicographic exponent group. A counterexample must do more than move the real axis,
since the strong phase twists of \cref{opa:cm:rem:gaussian} do that \src{14}. \emph{Status} (batch 31): open.

\begin{question}[Strongness of omnific embeddings \src{11, 12, 13, 14}]\label{opa:as:q:embeddingstrong}
Let $f:\No\hookrightarrow\No$ be a field embedding with $f^{-1}(\Oz)=\Oz$. Must $f$ be strongly additive? What
changes if also $\ct\circ f=\ct$? Without strongness, which coefficient and image restrictions survive; can such
an $f$ have a proper dense image, or one that is neither closed nor discrete?
\end{question}
The embeddings of \cref{opa:as:sec:explicit} are strong and do not answer the first clause; a counterexample
needs a summable family whose image is not summable or has the wrong sum, and the missing step in the isomorphism
proof is access to all target tests (\cref{opa:as:cor:image}) \src{12, 13}. \emph{Status.} The first two clauses
have a negative answer if there is a measurable cardinal, more generally an elementary embedding
$j:V\to M$ into a transitive class with a critical point, available as a class. The large-cardinal report
(\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}) shows that the map
$\hat\jmath$ obtained by applying $j$ to sign sequences is an ordered field embedding of $\No$ that fixes $\R$,
preserves and reflects $\Oz$ and preserves constant terms, while it sends a summable family to a summable family
whose sum is not the image of the sum; so it is not strongly additive. It is not onto, so it does not conflict
with \cref{opa:as:thm:main}. Consequently a positive answer is not provable in the base theory unless that theory
with a measurable cardinal is inconsistent; whether a counterexample exists without large cardinals stays open.
The necessary gap restriction of \cref{opa:as:thm:classification} and the dichotomy of
\cref{opa:as:thm:topology} remain valid without strongness, whereas the closed-image proof uses the strong
expansion formula. The questions on dense images and on images neither closed nor discrete are not settled here
\src{11}.

\begin{question}[Coefficient-fixing embeddings and target tests \src{13, 14}]\label{opa:as:q:targettests}
For $\R$-linear embeddings preserving the omnific predicate, can every target test $\ct(y\,\cdot\,)$ be represented,
after pullback, by a source multiplier?
\end{question}
Such adjoints suffice for strongness and are necessary at set size (\cref{opa:as:thm:adjoint}); the functional
$\lambda_{\On}$ shows that a proof for $\No$ must use the multiplicativity of the embedding, not a theorem about all
strong linear operators \src{13}. 14 asks which additional hypotheses make a constant-term-compatible embedding of full
Hahn fields strong, in particular for embeddings of $\No$ preserving the integer part. The isomorphism proof fails
exactly because a target detector need not lie in the image; at set size an adjoint of the underlying semilinear map
suffices, and 14 proposes seeking a criterion that the image supplies all detectors needed for its families, weaker
than surjectivity. No embedding theorem is deduced \src{14}.

\begin{question}[Coefficient-moving embeddings \src{12}; re-scoped]\label{opa:as:q:coefficientmoving}
Which subfields $C_0\subseteq\R$ occur as $f(\No)\cap\R$ for strongly additive embeddings $f$ with
$f^{-1}(\Oz)=\Oz$? Which arise from $\ker d$, and are higher-derivation coefficient lifts needed?
\end{question}
\emph{Partly answered} by 11: in the exact-monomial strong class the intersections are the fixed fields of the
coefficient sections $\rho$, which are real closed, and Taylor sections give $\ker d$
(\cref{opa:as:prop:overlap}). Which real closed subfields occur, and the classes with characters, unit corrections
or no monomial condition, stay open. A classification should separate the coefficient homomorphism, the value-group
embedding and the admissibility of their combination; block separation is sufficient, not necessary \src{12}.

\begin{question}[Removing exact monomial preservation \src{11}]\label{opa:as:q:units}
Classify the strong embeddings $f:\No\hookrightarrow\No$ preserving $\Oz$ with
$f(t^\gamma)=c_\gamma t^{\tau(\gamma)}(1+u_\gamma)$, $c_\gamma>0$, $v(u_\gamma)>0$. Which compatibility and summability
conditions are necessary and sufficient, and can every such embedding be normalized by an $\Oz$-preserving
automorphism?
\end{question}
The criterion of \cref{opa:part:aut} is a starting point, but surjectivity is unavailable: corrections on the image
need not extend to all target scales. A first step would keep the corrections on one common positive shift set
\src{11}.

\begin{question}[Elementarity of coefficient-fixing pair embeddings \src{11}]\label{opa:as:q:elementary}
For an ordered additive $\tau:\No\hookrightarrow\No$, when is $M_{1,\tau}:(\No,\Oz)\hookrightarrow(\No,\Oz)$
elementary? Is fixing $\R$ sufficient in the exact-monomial strong category?
\end{question}
\Cref{opa:as:thm:elementary} gives the necessary condition $\rho=\id$; quantifier elimination for real closed fields
cannot decide the expanded structure \src{11}.

\begin{question}[Coefficient sections \src{11}]\label{opa:as:q:sections}
Describe the ordered sections $\rho:\R\hookrightarrow\Hahn\R C$ with residue the identity. Which come from one Taylor
derivation, compositions of such, or summable families of higher derivations, and what distinguishes sections with the
same fixed subfield?
\end{question}
The classification reduces the coefficient part of the embedding problem exactly to this question; the one-sided
neighbourhood invariant is only a first invariant \src{11}.

\begin{question}[Conjugacy of proper copies \src{11, 13}]\label{opa:as:q:conjugacy}
Classify the images $J_{\tau,\rho}(\No)$ up to $\Oz$-preserving automorphisms of $\No$. How much do the value image, its
cuts, the fixed coefficient field and the one-sided neighbourhood data determine? When are two images $J_{\mu,d}(\No)$
conjugate, and which scale embeddings admit Taylor deformations with several independently prescribed motions?
\end{question}
\emph{Partly answered} by \cref{opa:as:thm:continuum}: the value image alone is insufficient, and continuum many types
occur in one field-conjugacy class. 13 notes that since every omnific automorphism fixes $\R$, the positions of the
deformed coefficient fields may give finer invariants; a restricted target is $\mathcal T_{d,\delta}\circ M_{1,\tau}$ for
fixed $\tau$ and one shift \src{11, 13}. \emph{Related} (batch 31): the independent-copies report asks the pair
version at a fixed Hahn core: which invariants decide whether an $\Oz$-preserving automorphism of $\No$ fixing
$\Hahn\R H$ carries one independent pair of copies with that common field to another (its second research question,
``Relative classification of pairs of copies'', unlabelled). It proves no conjugacy result, and the present
question stays open.

\begin{question}[Homogeneity of the pair \src{12, 13}]\label{opa:as:q:homogeneity}
Which isomorphisms between set-sized substructures of $(\No,\Oz)$ extend to automorphisms of the pair?
\end{question}
Fixing the reals of the domain is necessary (\cref{opa:as:cor:homogeneity}); no sufficiency theorem is proved. The
question differs from extension of elementary partial maps in a first-order language. A first target is a
back-and-forth theorem for truncation-closed Hahn subfields with prescribed real coefficients and compatible integer
parts \src{12, 13}.

\begin{question}[Surcomplex embeddings without a real form \src{11}]\label{opa:as:q:surcomplexembed}
Classify the embeddings of $(\SC,\Og)$ without assuming commutation with conjugation, and decide which of the
nonconjugate copies of \cref{opa:as:cor:complexcopies} stay nonconjugate under the larger group.
\end{question}
$\Og$ reconstructs $\C$ but gives no pointwise real rigidity for the sign invariant \src{11}.

\begin{question}[The proper-class strong dual \src{12, 14}]\label{opa:as:q:classdual}
Can the strong $\R$-linear dual of $\No$ be described by class coefficient functionals meeting every well-ordered set
finitely, and which maps have transposes that stay set-supported on every argument?
\end{question}
Such a description must avoid unlicensed collections of classes; the issue is foundational as well as algebraic
\src{12}. 14 asks for a natural enlargement of surreal kernels representing every strong $\R$-linear functional on
$\No$: formal kernels supported on a proper class whose pairing with every set-sized normal form is finite, which
recover the set-sized duality on restriction and explain which strong class operators have surreal-valued adjoints.
Such kernels are not surreal numbers, and merely writing a proper-class sum is not a definition \src{14}.

\begin{question}[Singular support bounds \src{12, 13}]\label{opa:as:q:singular}
For singular uncountable $\kappa$, when does a coefficient- and constant-term-compatible automorphism of
$K_{<\kappa}$ preserve summability with total support of size $<\kappa$?
\end{question}
Countable binary witnesses lie in $K_{<\kappa}$ and detect well-ordering and local finiteness, but not the cardinal
bound on unions; a sharp theorem may need uniform bounds on supports of monomial images, and a counterexample must
preserve the restricted fields themselves \src{12, 13}. \emph{Partial information} (batch 31): by 14's detection in
subfields (\cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace,opa:cm:rem:kappa}) such an automorphism sends every family summable in
$K_{<\kappa}$ to a Hahn-summable family with sum the image of the sum; only the size of the union of the image
supports remains. The question stays open.

\begin{question}[Other rings and coefficient fields \src{12, 14}; \mergetag]\label{opa:as:q:otherrings}
For which integer parts, truncation subrings or distinguished subrings of a real closed Hahn field can one reconstruct a
scalar pairing that forces automatic strongness of their stabilizers? In particular, for which characteristic-zero $k$
and unital $\oo\subseteq k$ does $\sigma(\Ro)=\Ro$ force a field automorphism of $\Hahn k\Gamma$ to be strong?
\end{question}
The omnific proof uses a divisible ideal, its multiplier ring and units to recover coefficients, then the order to
recover the infinitesimals; it would be useful to find the weakest hypotheses under which this works and examples of
failure \src{12}. The merge adds the second sentence: for $\oo=k$ the divisible-ideal step fails, and for non-real $k$
without valuation data the order step is missing; compare \cref{opa:sc:q:otherparts}. 14 asks the same for integer
parts $A$ of a real closed Hahn field, and whether the full constant term is reconstructible from $A$ by the same chain;
it suggests the checkable sufficient condition that the divisibility core of $A$ recover a negative-support complement
whose multiplier units form the coefficient field, and asks when that condition is necessary and stable under
conjugation \src{14}.

\begin{question}[Isomorphisms of small integer parts \src{13, 14}]\label{opa:as:q:smallparts}
For which ordered groups $\Gamma,\Delta$ does every ring isomorphism $\mathcal R_\Gamma\cong\mathcal R_\Delta$ extend to
an isomorphism of the full real Hahn fields preserving order and integer parts?
\end{question}
\Cref{opa:as:ex:smallfrac} shows the fraction field is not the Hahn field in general. One route is an intrinsic
summation completion compatible with the pairing; another is to find two Hahn fields with indistinguishable small
integer parts \src{13}. 14 asks the automorphism case for divisible $\Gamma$, with uniqueness: first determine
$\Frac\mathcal R_\Gamma$ and its position in $K_\Gamma$, since extension through the remaining series is a separate
problem \src{14}. \emph{Status} (batch 31): an extension, if it exists, is unique (\cref{opa:cm:prop:unique},
\mergetag); existence stays open.

\begin{question}[Exponential, omega-map and differential structure \src{11, 12, 13}]\label{opa:as:q:exp}
Which proper embeddings preserving $\Oz$, and which gap sections and value embeddings, are compatible with the surreal
exponential, the omega-map, a surreal derivation or a simplicity relation? Intersect the full omnific stabilizer with the
automorphisms preserving such a structure.
\end{question}
The Taylor block embeddings give flexibility only in the algebraic pair and fail omega-map compatibility at $1$; the
questions must be compared with those of \cite{KKS}, not inferred from the construction, and the exponential rigidity
already in the collection is not to be re-solved \src{11, 12, 13}.

\begin{question}[Restricted workspaces \src{11}]\label{opa:as:q:workspaces}
For a support-restricted subfield of a full Hahn field, when does the bottom-gap extension stay inside it, and which
restrictions make the canonical integer part have the whole workspace as fraction field?
\end{question}
The block construction preserves well-ordering but not bounds on support cardinality, order type or boundedness; this
matters for birthday cutoffs, computable presentations and transseries subfields \src{11}.

\begin{question}[Compositions \src{11}]\label{opa:as:q:composition}
Describe composition and image inclusion of classified maps in terms of the data $(\tau,\rho)$; the composite of
$(\tau_1,\rho_1)$ and $(\tau_2,\rho_2)$ has exponent map $\tau_2\tau_1$ and section $J_{\tau_2,\rho_2}\circ\rho_1$. Can
image inclusion and ambient extendability be decided from smaller invariants?
\end{question}
\emph{Status} (batch 31): image inclusion is settled in a special case. Take $\rho=\id$ and the exponent ranges
$G_i=h_i(\No)$, $i\in I$, of the family in the independent-copies report, which fix a common set-sized divisible
group $H$ and are independent over it (\lbl{isc:thm:group}). Put $E_J=\Hahn\R{G_J}$ with $G_J=H+\sum_{j\in J}G_j$, so
that $E_{\{i\}}=J_{h_i,\id}(\No)$. That report proves $E_J\cap E_T=E_{J\cap T}$, and $E_J\subseteq E_T$ exactly when
$J\subseteq T$ (\lbl{isc:thm:boolean}). This decides inclusion and intersection among these images and their full
Hahn joins. Composition is not treated there, and the general question stays open.

\begin{question}[Formalization \src{11, 12, 13, 14}]\label{opa:as:q:formal}
Formalize the finite-intersection lemma, the triangular or binary detector, the detection theorem and the
constant-term isomorphism theorem for arbitrary full Hahn fields; then the multiplier reconstruction and the omnific
specialization; and, separately, the bottom-gap classification for set-sized Hahn fields, the closed-image proof and the
two-level index lift, isolating the class-size assumptions for $\No$.
\end{question}
The sources propose this order; finite checks remain a separate test layer. Nothing in this part is formalized in Lean
\src{11, 12, 13}. 14 proposes the same order, beginning with an abstract matrix lemma with finite row and column
supports (\cref{opa:cm:lem:matching}), and asks that the full-surreal corollary go through a stated universe-relative
or class-coded normal-form interface, not by treating $\No$ as a set, and that the failure of class duality
(\cref{opa:as:thm:classdefect}) be formalized separately; its proposed module names are plans only \src{14}.

\part{A fourth derivation: induced matchings, detector subfields and formal evaluation}\label{opa:cm:part}

This part records manuscript 14, the addition of batch 31. It proves again the main
theorems of \cref{opa:part:as} for the real pair, in less generality; those results are
printed once, in \cref{opa:part:as}, with 14 added to their sources, and
\cref{opa:cm:sub:once} lists them. What is printed here is what 14 adds: a third proof of
scalar detection, by induced matchings, whose witness has all coefficients equal to $1$
and needs no choice of coefficients (\cref{opa:cm:sec:matching}); detection inside subfields
that contain enough such witnesses (\cref{opa:cm:sec:workspaces}); and equivariance of
formal power-series evaluation, the general adjoint-matrix formula, the rank-one group
$\Gamma=\R$ and the source's shear and truncation examples
(\cref{opa:cm:sec:further}). Its labels carry the sub-prefix \lbl{opa:cm:}.

\section{Source 14, what it re-derives, and its notation}\label{opa:cm:sec:intro}

\subsection{The manuscript and its pin}\label{opa:cm:sub:source}

Manuscript 14 is \emph{Automatic Summability from Omnific Arithmetic: constant-term duality,
the full omnific stabilizer, and a sharp set--class distinction}, dated 23 September 2026,
24 pages. It is item 07 of batch 31, delivered as the archive
\fn{omnific_automatic_summability} in commit \texttt{39fe674} and placed as an addition in
commit \texttt{9d28e28}; it is numbered 14 here after its file prefix, continuing this
report's local sequence. Its finite checks, their recorded run and its source statement are
shipped as \fn{code/14-automatic-summability-verify.py},
\fn{data/14-automatic-summability-verification.json} and
\fn{14-automatic-summability-SOURCES.md}; its \fn{article.tex}, PDF, README and checksum list
are not shipped.

It pins repository commit \texttt{3d40856}. That commit contains this report's
\fn{article.tex} exactly as written in batch 26 (\texttt{9b80a30}), that is
\cref{opa:part:aut,opa:part:par} without the material of source 10, together with the
placement \texttt{c6359e4} of source 10's files. It contains neither \texttt{b3fa9e2}, the
batch-28 write of source 10, nor \texttt{21375f8} and \texttt{20c4c9f}, the placement and
the write of sources 11--13. So 14's author saw \cref{opa:q:strong} open and saw none of
\cref{opa:part:as}; the three batch-30 authors and 14 worked independently of one another.
14 describes the batch-26 report accurately: its criterion concerns strong
coefficient-fixing $1$-automorphisms, and 14 credits its reconstruction and its
convex-scale criterion as antecedents.

\subsection{Printed once}\label{opa:cm:sub:once}

The table maps 14's statements to the results where they are printed. ``Same'' means the
same statement with at most notational differences; otherwise the difference is named.
14's theorem numbers are those of its own PDF.
