So 14 is a third independent derivation of the automatic-strongness theorem, after 12 and
13, and adds nothing to its generality. Its route
$\mathcal R_\Gamma\to\Pi\to\mathcal A_\Gamma\to\R\to\mm\to\ct\to$ summation is the chain
printed after \cref{opa:as:thm:main}. Where 14's proof of a shared result differs only in
presentation it is not reprinted. Its genuinely different proof, the induced matching, is
kept as a marked third route.

\subsection{Conventions and notation of this part}\label{opa:cm:sub:notation}

This part uses the conventions of \cref{opa:as:sub:notation}: the small-$t$ convention
$t^\gamma=\omega^{-\gamma}$, $K_\Gamma=\Hahn k\Gamma$, $\Pi_\Gamma$, $\mathcal R_\Gamma=\Z\oplus\Pi_\Gamma$,
$\mathcal A_\Gamma=\R\oplus\Pi_\Gamma$ and the pairing $\pairing xy=\ct(xy)$. Manuscript 14 also
writes in the $t$-convention. Its symbols are renamed as follows.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.27\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.65\textwidth}}
\toprule
14's symbol & Here\\
\midrule
\endhead
$A_\Gamma=\Z\oplus\Pi$, and $A$ & $\mathcal R_\Gamma$; for $\Gamma=\No$, $\Oz$.\\
$B=k\oplus\Pi$ & $\Ak$; for $k=\R$, $\mathcal A_\Gamma$.\\
$\langle x,y\rangle$ & $\pairing xy$.\\
$\tau:k\to l$, the coefficient isomorphism & $\alpha$, as in \cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso}.\\
$\phi$, the exponent automorphism; $M_{\chi,\phi}$ & $\tau$; $M_{\chi,\tau}$ of \eqref{opa:eq:monomialmap}.\\
$\rho$, a ring automorphism of $\Oz$; $\widetilde\rho$ & $\sigma$ and $\widetilde\sigma$, as in \cref{opa:as:thm:main}.\\
$\nu$, a normalized $1$-automorphism; $\mathcal U$, $\mathcal G$, $\mathcal M$ & $u$; $\UU_{\mathcal R_\Z}(\Gamma)$
(and $\UU_\Oz$); the stabilizer of $\mathcal R_\Gamma$; the monomial maps.\\
$L_\nu(g)$, $a_{\nu,\delta}$ & $h_u(g)$, $a_{u,\delta}$ of \eqref{opa:eq:logchar}.\\
$T^*$ & $T^\dagger$; the matrix $T_{h,g}=\coef h{T(t^g)}$ keeps its name.\\
$\Lambda$ & $\lambda_{\On}$ of \eqref{opa:as:eq:classfunctional}; 14's $[\omega^\alpha]x$ is $\coef{-\alpha}x$.\\
$F$, a power series & $\mathsf F$.\\
$F$, a subfield & $K'$.\\
$P_{<\gamma}$, truncation & $T_{<\gamma}$, as in \cref{opa:thm:projections}.\\
$e_1,e_2$ of its shear ($e_1$ the larger scale); $s=t^{e_2}$ & $e_2,e_1$ of \cref{opa:ex:Qr} ($e_1\bb e_2$);
$s$ is written $t^{e_1}$.\\
$h$, an additive map & $\theta$.\\
$u_1,\dots,u_m$, $u$, infinitesimals & $z_1,\dots,z_m$, $z$; $u$ is the leading-term-fixing factor.\\
$d>0$ in its cancellation example & $\delta>0$; $d$ is a derivation of $\R$ in \cref{opa:part:as}.\\
$\mathbf{No}[i]$, $\mathbf{Oz}[i]$; $A_\C$, $K_\C$, $\Pi_\C$, $B_\C$ & $\SC$, $\Og$;
$\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma$, $K^\C_\Gamma$, $\Pi^\C_\Gamma$, $\C\oplus\Pi^\C_\Gamma$.\\
$L=l((u^\Delta))$ & $L=\Hahn l\Delta$, with $t$ for the monomials of both fields.\\
$a\ll b$ & $a\bb b$, the same relation.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The collisions are resolved here only, and the tempting false readings are wrong:
\begin{itemize}[itemsep=1pt]
\item 14's $A_\Gamma$ is \emph{not} $\mathcal A_\Gamma$: 14's $A_\Gamma=\Z\oplus\Pi$ is
$\mathcal R_\Gamma$, and $\mathcal A_\Gamma=\R\oplus\Pi$ is 14's $B$.
\item 14's $\tau$ is a coefficient isomorphism; $\tau$ here is the exponent automorphism,
14's $\phi$. 14's $\rho$ is a ring automorphism of $\Oz$; $\rho$ in \cref{opa:part:as} is a
coefficient section.
\item 14's $F$ has two meanings, neither of which is the isomorphism $F$ of
\cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso} or the profile $F$ of \eqref{opa:sc:eq:binomial}.
\item In 14's shear $e_1$ is the \emph{larger} scale; in \cref{opa:ex:Qr} it is the smaller. The
letters are swapped here.
\item 14's truncation $P_{<\gamma}$ is not one of the zero-cut projections $P_-,P_0,P_+$.
\item 14's additive $h$ is not the logarithmic character $h_\sigma$.
\end{itemize}
14 calls an additive map \emph{strong} when it sends summable families to summable families
with the same sum; this is ``strongly additive'' here, and 14 states both directions where it
asserts them. Its ``normalized $1$-automorphism'' is a $1$-automorphism in the sense of
\cref{opa:sub:notation}; 14 does not assume it strong, and by \cref{opa:as:cor:setpairs}
every one preserving $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ is.

\section{Induced matchings: a third route to scalar detection}\label{opa:cm:sec:matching}

\begin{lemma}[An infinite induced matching \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:lem:matching}
Let $(a_{n,j})_{n\in\N,\,j\in I}$ be a matrix over a field, $I$ a set. Suppose that every row
is nonzero and has finite support, and that every column has finite support. Then there are
row indices $n_0<n_1<\cdots$ and distinct column indices $j_0,j_1,\ldots$ with
\[
 a_{n_r,j_r}\ne0,\qquad a_{n_r,j_s}=0\quad(r\ne s).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose a nonzero entry in row $n_0=0$, in column $j_0$. After $r$ choices let $E$ be the union
of the supports of the chosen rows, a finite set of columns. Only finitely many rows meet $E$,
since each column is finite. Choose a row $n_r$ beyond all earlier chosen rows that does not
meet $E$, and a nonzero entry in it, in column $j_r$. Then $j_r\notin E$, so column $j_r$
vanishes on the earlier chosen rows, and row $n_r$ vanishes on the earlier chosen columns,
which lie in $E$. Induction gives the matching. The selection uses only the pattern of
nonzero entries, not the characteristic or the values of the coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Third route to \textup{(iii)$\Rightarrow$(i)} in \cref{opa:as:thm:detect} \src{14}]
Assume (iii). The tests $y=t^{-g}$ give pointwise finiteness. Suppose the union $S$ of the
supports is not well ordered, and choose $g_0>g_1>\cdots$ in $S$. Put
$a_{n,j}=\coef{g_n}{x_j}$. Row $n$ is nonzero because $g_n\in S$, and finite by pointwise
finiteness. Column $j$ is finite, since infinitely many $g_n$ in $\supp x_j$ would descend in
a well-ordered set. \Cref{opa:cm:lem:matching} gives $n_r$ and $j_r$; put
\begin{equation}\label{opa:cm:eq:allones}
 y=\sum_{r\ge0}t^{-g_{n_r}} ,
\end{equation}
a Hahn series, since its exponents increase, with countable support and every coefficient
equal to $1$. Then $\pairing{x_{j_r}}y=\sum_{s\ge0}\coef{g_{n_s}}{x_{j_r}}=a_{n_r,j_r}\ne0$ for every
$r$: the sum is finite, and only its term $s=r$ is nonzero. The distinct $j_r$ contradict
(iii).
\end{proof}

The three routes differ in how cancellation is prevented. 12's triangular detector
(\cref{opa:as:lem:triangular}) makes the family triangular and then chooses coefficients in
$\{0,1\}$ one at a time; 13's binary lemma (\cref{opa:as:lem:binary}) keeps the family and
chooses coefficients row by row; 14 passes to an induced matching, a diagonal submatrix, so
each selected pairing is one matrix entry and nothing is chosen. 14's witness is therefore a
special case of the tests in (iii): countable support and all coefficients $1$. Every
construction uses a countable subset of one set-sized support, so the route works for
$\Gamma=\No$ and for $\SC$ \src{14}.

\begin{corollary}[Monomial and all-ones tests suffice \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:cor:allones}
A set-indexed family $(x_j)$ in a full Hahn field $K=\Hahn k\Gamma$, $\Gamma$ a set or $\No$, is
Hahn summable if and only if every exponent lies in only finitely many supports and, for
every strictly increasing sequence $(h_n)_{n\in\N}$ in $\Gamma$, only finitely many $j$ have
$\pairing{x_j}{\sum_nt^{h_n}}\ne0$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Necessity is (i)$\Rightarrow$(ii) of \cref{opa:as:thm:detect}. For sufficiency the third route
uses only monomial tests and the series \eqref{opa:cm:eq:allones}, whose exponents
$-g_{n_r}$ increase strictly.
\end{proof}

14 states this through the proof of its detection theorem and its Proposition~11.3
(\cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace}).

\begin{example}[A nonexistent sum disguised as zero \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:ex:cancellation}
Let $\delta>0$ in $\Gamma$ and, for $n\ge1$, $x_{n,+}=t^{-n\delta}$ and $x_{n,-}=-t^{-n\delta}$. Each exponent
lies in exactly two members, and grouping the members in pairs would cancel every
coefficient. The family is not Hahn summable, since $\{-n\delta:n\ge1\}$ descends. The all-ones
series $y=\sum_{n\ge1}t^{n\delta}$ gives $\pairing{x_{n,+}}y=1$ and $\pairing{x_{n,-}}y=-1$ for every
$n$: the test fails before any cancellation can hide the infinitely many contributions.
\end{example}

Here each exponent occurs twice with cancelling coefficients, whereas in
\cref{opa:as:ex:monomialtests} each exponent occurs once. The detection theorem recognizes
admissibility of the family, not a value assigned by some other summation convention;
neither conditional convergence nor an ordering of the index set is used \src{14}.

\begin{remark}[Summability is not convergence \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:rem:topology}
The family $(t^{n/(n+1)})_{n\ge1}$ is Hahn summable over $\Q$, since its exponents increase,
but its terms do not tend to zero in the valuation topology. A valuation-preserving
automorphism is continuous, but continuity speaks only about families that converge in that
topology, and the detector also handles bounded increasing supports; for $\No$, set-indexed
topological convergence meets further size obstructions. The detection theorem reduces
summability to scalar product tests, without completeness or net arguments. This agrees
with \cref{opa:as:rem:finiteness}. 14 identifies the dual supports, the sets meeting every
well-ordered set finitely, with the reverse-well-ordered sets, as the total-order case of
\cite[Theorem~3 and Lemmas~4.1--4.2]{BCJS}, and claims no origin of that duality.
\end{remark}

\section{Detection in subfields with enough detectors}\label{opa:cm:sec:workspaces}

\begin{definition}[Countable detector property \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:def:detector}
Let $K'$ be a subfield of $K=\Hahn k\Gamma$, $\Gamma$ a set or $\No$, containing $k$ and every
monomial. $K'$ has the \emph{countable detector property} if $\sum_{n\ge0}t^{h_n}\in K'$ for every
strictly increasing sequence $(h_n)_{n\in\N}$ in $\Gamma$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Detection in a subfield \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:prop:workspace}
Let $K'$ have the countable detector property.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},itemsep=1pt]
\item A set-indexed family in $K'$ is Hahn summable in $K$ if and only if, for every $y\in K'$,
only finitely many $\pairing{x_j}y$ are nonzero.
\item Let $\sigma$ be a field automorphism of $K'$ with $\sigma(k)=k$ and
$\ct(\sigma x)=\alpha(\ct x)$ for $x\in K'$, where $\alpha=\sigma|_k$. If $(x_j)$ in $K'$ is Hahn
summable in $K$, so is $(\sigma(x_j))$. If moreover $s=\sum_jx_j$ lies in $K'$, then
$\sum_j\sigma(x_j)=\sigma(s)$; in particular this sum lies in $K'$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) Necessity holds because $K'\subseteq K$ (\cref{opa:as:thm:detect}). The tests of
\cref{opa:cm:cor:allones} are monomials and series \eqref{opa:cm:eq:allones}, which lie in $K'$;
this gives sufficiency.
(b) For $y\in K'$ write $y=\sigma(z)$ with $z\in K'$. Then
$\pairing{\sigma x_j}y=\ct(\sigma(x_jz))=\alpha(\pairing{x_j}z)$, nonzero for finitely many $j$ by
(a), so $(\sigma(x_j))$ is summable in $K$ by (a). Let $s'$ be its sum in $K$. By
\eqref{opa:as:eq:pairingsum} in $K$, for every $y\in K'$,
\[
 \pairing{\sigma(s)}y=\alpha(\pairing sz)=\sum_j\alpha(\pairing{x_j}z)=\sum_j\pairing{\sigma x_j}y
 =\pairing{s'}y,
\]
with finite sums. So $\sigma(s)-s'$ pairs to zero with every $y\in K'$, in particular with every
monomial, and $\sigma(s)=s'$.
\end{proof}

The inverse satisfies the same hypotheses with $\alpha^{-1}$. As in the proof of
\cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso}, the hypothesis $\sigma(k)=k$ may be replaced by covariance
$\ct(\sigma x)=\alpha(\ct x)$ for some field automorphism $\alpha$ of $k$: since $K'$ contains
every monomial the pairing separates $K'$, and $\sigma(r)=\alpha(r)$ follows for $r\in k$
\mergetag. The proposition says nothing about sums that leave $K'$, and it concerns the
summation of the ambient full Hahn field, not a summation intrinsic to $K'$.

\begin{example}[Puiseux series have too few detectors \mergetag]\label{opa:cm:ex:puiseux}
Let $K=\Hahn\R\Q$ and let $K'=\bigcup_{N\ge1}\Hahn\R{\tfrac1N\Z}$ be the field of Puiseux series; it
contains $\R$ and every monomial. The family $x_n=t^{-n/(n+1)}$, $n\ge1$, is not Hahn summable
in $K$, since its exponents descend. For $y\in K'$ with $\supp y\subseteq\frac1N\Z$,
$\pairing{x_n}y=\coef{n/(n+1)}y$ is nonzero only if $Nn/(n+1)\in\Z$, that is only if $n+1$
divides $N$, since $n$ and $n+1$ are coprime. So every test in $K'$ gives finitely many nonzero
pairings, and (a) of \cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace} fails for $K'$. Accordingly
$\sum_nt^{n/(n+1)}$, the sum of the summable family of \cref{opa:cm:rem:topology}, is not a
Puiseux series.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[Relation to support-bounded fields \mergetag]\label{opa:cm:rem:kappa}
For every uncountable cardinal $\kappa$, regular or singular, the field $K_{<\kappa}$ of
\cref{opa:as:prop:kappa} has the countable detector property. So
\cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace} gives for all such $\kappa$ what \cref{opa:as:prop:kappa} gives for
regular $\kappa$ as far as ambient summability goes: a coefficient- and constant-term-compatible
automorphism of $K_{<\kappa}$ sends every family that is summable in the sense of $K_{<\kappa}$ to a
family that is Hahn summable in $K$, with fewer than $\kappa$ nonzero members and with sum the
image of the sum. For singular $\kappa$ what \cref{opa:as:q:singular} still asks is only
whether the union of the image supports has size $<\kappa$. The warning of
\cref{opa:as:rem:checklist}, that the detector must lie in the field tested and that this
fails for arbitrary subfields, stands: \cref{opa:cm:ex:puiseux} is such a failure, and the
countable detector property is a sufficient condition, not a characterization
(\cref{opa:cm:q:closure}). 14 does not claim the property for transseries, Puiseux or
left-finite subfields; it notes that a series on an increasing sequence of exponents bounded
above can fail a left-finiteness restriction \src{14}. \Cref{opa:as:q:workspaces} (11) asks
the analogous question for the bottom-gap embeddings.
\end{remark}

\section{Further consequences}\label{opa:cm:sec:further}

\begin{theorem}[Formal evaluation is transported \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:thm:formal}
Let $\Gamma$ be $\No$ or a nonzero divisible set-sized ordered abelian group, and let $\sigma$
be a field automorphism of $K_\Gamma=\Hahn\R\Gamma$ with $\sigma(\mathcal R_\Gamma)=\mathcal R_\Gamma$.
For $\mathsf F\in\R[[X_1,\dots,X_m]]$ and $z_1,\dots,z_m\in\mm_\Gamma$,
\begin{equation}\label{opa:cm:eq:formal}
 \sigma\bigl(\mathsf F(z_1,\dots,z_m)\bigr)=\mathsf F\bigl(\sigma(z_1),\dots,\sigma(z_m)\bigr).
\end{equation}
In particular $\sigma(\exp z)=\exp(\sigma z)$ and $\sigma(\log(1+z))=\log(1+\sigma z)$ for
$z\in\mm_\Gamma$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Write $\mathsf F=\sum_\nu c_\nu X^\nu$ over $\nu\in\N^m$. The union of the supports of the $z_j$ is
a well-ordered subset of $\Gamma^{>0}$, so the Neumann support principle
(\cref{opa:sec:prelim}) makes $(c_\nu z^\nu)_\nu$ Hahn summable; its sum is the formal evaluation.
By \cref{opa:as:prop:setpairs,opa:as:cor:setpairs} (and \cref{opa:as:thm:main} for $\No$),
$\sigma$ fixes $\R$, maps $\mm_\Gamma$ onto itself and is strongly $\R$-linear, so
$\sigma(\sum_\nu c_\nu z^\nu)=\sum_\nu c_\nu\sigma(z)^\nu$, and the $\sigma(z_j)$ are again
infinitesimal.
\end{proof}

No radius of convergence enters: a formal series with arbitrarily fast-growing real
coefficients is evaluated at Hahn infinitesimals through support finiteness. For a
valuation-compatible Gaussian automorphism as in \cref{opa:as:thm:gaussian}, with
$\alpha=\sigma|_\C$, the same proof gives $\sigma(\mathsf F(z))=(\alpha\mathsf F)(\sigma(z))$, where
$\alpha$ acts on the coefficients; for the conjugation-compatible automorphisms of
\cref{opa:as:thm:conjugation}, $\alpha$ is the identity or complex conjugation \src{14}. The
exponential here is the local one at infinitesimals (\cref{opa:sec:prelim}); nothing is
claimed about the Gonshor exponential of infinite arguments, whose compatibility is a
further condition studied in \cite{KKS}.

\begin{proposition}[The adjoint as a sign-reversed transpose \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:prop:adjointmatrix}
In the setting of \cref{opa:as:sec:adjoint}, let $T:K\to L$ be strongly $k$-linear and put
$T_{\eta,\gamma}=\coef\eta{T(t^\gamma)}$ for $\gamma\in\Gamma$, $\eta\in\Delta$. Then for every
$y=\sum_\eta y_\eta t^\eta\in L$ and $\gamma\in\Gamma$
\begin{equation}\label{opa:cm:eq:adjointmatrix}
 \coef\gamma{T^\dagger(y)}=\sum_{\eta\in\Delta}T_{-\eta,-\gamma}\,y_\eta ,\qquad\text{so}\qquad
 (T^\dagger)_{\gamma,\eta}=T_{-\eta,-\gamma},
\end{equation}
with finitely many nonzero terms.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$\coef\gamma{T^\dagger(y)}=\pairing{t^{-\gamma}}{T^\dagger y}=\pairing{T(t^{-\gamma})}y$ by
\eqref{opa:as:eq:adjoint}, and the last constant term is
$\sum_\eta\coef{-\eta}{T(t^{-\gamma})}\,y_\eta$, finite by \cref{opa:as:lem:intersection}.
\end{proof}

For a coefficient-fixing isomorphism $F$ with $\ct\circ F=\ct$ one has $F^\dagger=F^{-1}$
(\cref{opa:as:thm:adjoint}), and \eqref{opa:cm:eq:adjointmatrix} becomes the coefficient-matrix
formula \eqref{opa:as:eq:matrix} of 13. The general formula is 14's; it presupposes the
strongness of $T$, which \cref{opa:as:thm:adjoint} supplies, and 14's suite checks it only on
finite matrices (\cref{opa:app:checks}).

\begin{remark}[Rank one with $\Gamma=\R$ \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:rem:real}
By \cref{opa:as:cor:arch} every field automorphism of $\Hahn\R\R$ preserving $\mathcal R_\R$ is
a monomial map $M_{\chi,\tau}$. Here $\tau(\gamma)=\lambda\gamma$ with $\lambda>0$, since an
order-preserving additive bijection of $\R$ is monotone and additive, hence $\R$-linear; and
$\chi(\gamma)=\exp(\theta(\gamma))$ with $\theta:\R\to\R$ additive, since $\chi$ is positive.
$\theta$ need not be continuous: any $\Q$-linear map defined on a Hamel basis will do, and
every such pair $(\lambda,\theta)$ gives an automorphism by \eqref{opa:eq:monomialmap}. So
rank one removes the factor $u$, not all automorphisms and not all nonclassical coefficient
scalings. For $\Gamma=\Q$ the character is $q\mapsto c^q$ (the paragraph after
\cref{opa:thm:factorization}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The shear of source 14 and truncation \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:rem:shear}
Let $\Gamma=\Q e_1\oplus\Q e_2$ with $e_1\bb e_2$ as in \cref{opa:ex:Qr}, and let $\theta$ be the
$e_2$-coordinate. 14's automorphism, in these letters,
\[
 t^{ae_2+be_1}\longmapsto t^{ae_2+be_1}(1+t^{e_1})^a\qquad(a,b\in\Q),
\]
is the binomial twist $\sigma^{1+\xi}_{e_1,\theta}$ of \eqref{opa:sc:eq:binomialone}, so it is a strong
$\R$-linear $1$-automorphism preserving $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ (\cref{opa:sc:thm:twists}, since
$\theta(C_{e_1})=0$); it is the inverse of \cref{opa:sc:ex:ranktwo}. It sends $t^{-e_2}$ to
$\sum_{n\ge0}(-1)^nt^{-e_2+ne_1}$, a monomial to an infinite series. With $\gamma=-e_2+\frac12e_1$
and $T_{<\gamma}$ the truncation below $\gamma$,
\[
 T_{<\gamma}\bigl(\sigma(t^{-e_2})\bigr)=t^{-e_2},\qquad
 \sigma\bigl(T_{<\gamma}(t^{-e_2})\bigr)=\sigma(t^{-e_2})\ne t^{-e_2}.
\]
So automatic strongness does not give commutation with truncation at a monomial, even when
the exponent automorphism is the identity. This does not contradict \cref{opa:thm:projections}:
the definable truncation $T_{<\gamma}(f)=mP_-(f/m)$ uses the parameter $m=t^\gamma$, and
$\sigma$ carries it to $f\mapsto\sigma(m)P_-(f/\sigma(m))$, truncation relative to
$\sigma(m)=t^\gamma(1+t^{e_1})^{-1}$, which is not a monomial. The tempting reading ``every
$\Oz$-preserving automorphism commutes with truncation at every monomial'' is false; the true
statements are commutation with $P_-,P_0,P_+$ (\cref{opa:cor:allautos}) and with the
projection onto any union of signed Archimedean blocks (\cref{opa:sc:prop:blocks}), in
agreement with the paragraph after that proposition. 14 relates naming truncations to the
complexity studied by Camacho \cite{Camacho}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Extensions from $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ are unique \mergetag]\label{opa:cm:prop:unique}
Let $\Gamma$ be a nonzero divisible set-sized ordered abelian group. A ring automorphism $\sigma$ of
$\mathcal R_\Gamma$ has at most one extension to a field automorphism of $K_\Gamma$; an extension,
if one exists, fixes $\R$ and is strongly $\R$-linear with strongly $\R$-linear inverse.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
An extension $\widetilde\sigma$ satisfies $\widetilde\sigma(\mathcal R_\Gamma)=\sigma(\mathcal R_\Gamma)=\mathcal R_\Gamma$,
so by \cref{opa:as:cor:setpairs} it fixes $\R$ and is strongly $\R$-linear with strong inverse. Its
values on monomials are forced: $\widetilde\sigma(t^\gamma)=\sigma(t^\gamma)$ for $\gamma<0$, since
$t^\gamma\in\Pi_\Gamma$, and $\widetilde\sigma(t^\gamma)=\sigma(t^{-\gamma})^{-1}$ for $\gamma>0$. Applied to
the normal-form family $(a_\gamma t^\gamma)$ of any $f$, strongness gives
$\widetilde\sigma(f)=\sum_\gamma a_\gamma\widetilde\sigma(t^\gamma)$, which depends only on $\sigma$.
\end{proof}

This answers the uniqueness clause of 14's Question~12.7 without assuming
$\Frac\mathcal R_\Gamma=K_\Gamma$, which fails in general (\cref{opa:as:ex:smallfrac}); 14 notes
only that uniqueness on the fraction field is automatic. Existence is
\cref{opa:as:q:smallparts}.

\begin{remark}[Gaussian statements of source 14 \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:rem:gaussian}
14's real-structure theorem assumes $\sigma(\No)=\No$, where \cref{opa:as:thm:conjugation}
assumes commutation with conjugation. The hypotheses are equivalent: conjugation fixes exactly
$\No$, and conversely $\sigma(i)=\pm i$ gives $\sigma(x+iy)=\sigma(x)\pm i\sigma(y)$, which commutes
with conjugation when $\sigma(\No)=\No$. 14's example over $\Gamma=\Q$, the strong map
$\sum a_qt^q\mapsto\sum a_qe^{i\pi q/2}t^q$, preserves $\mathcal R^\C_\Q$, fixes $\C$ and sends
$t$ to $it$; on $\SC$ it uses $\chi(\gamma)=\exp(i\pi\theta(\gamma)/2)$ with $\theta(\gamma)=\ct(\gamma)$.
It is a phase twist of the kind recorded after \cref{opa:thm:realaxis}
(\lbl{odg:def:lem:twist}, \lbl{saut:thm:phase}). Since it is strong, it does not bear on
\cref{opa:as:q:gaussian}; it only shows that preservation of $\Og$ alone does not give the
hypothesis of \cref{opa:as:thm:conjugation}.
\end{remark}

\section{Questions of source 14, and stale statements}\label{opa:cm:sec:questions}

\subsection{Status of the source's questions}\label{opa:cm:sub:status}

14 poses eight questions (its 12.1--12.8), proposed as research problems, not as published
open problems, and repeats its Gaussian question at the end of its Section~10. Seven are
merged into questions already in the report, with 14 added to their sources:
\begin{itemize}[itemsep=2pt]
\item 12.1 and the Section-10 question (must every automorphism of $\SC$ preserving $\Og$ be
strong; does $\Og$ determine the valuation ring, or at least $\ct$-equivariance?):
\cref{opa:as:q:gaussian}, open; 14's suggested first targets are recorded there.
\item 12.2 (which hypotheses make a constant-term-compatible embedding strong; embeddings of
$\No$ preserving $\Oz$): \cref{opa:as:q:embeddingstrong,opa:as:q:targettests}. The first
clauses of the former have a negative answer given a measurable cardinal, as recorded there.
\item 12.4 (class-supported kernels for strong functionals on $\No$): \cref{opa:as:q:classdual}.
\item 12.5 (other integer parts): \cref{opa:as:q:otherrings}, and \cref{opa:sc:q:otherparts}
for the criterion.
\item 12.6 (a parameter space for the normalized factor): \cref{opa:q:characters}, with
\cref{opa:q:generation}; open. 14 adds that the convex criterion recognizes existing
automorphisms and does not construct them, and that a parameterization must distinguish
pointwise positive support from uniform control on summable families.
\item 12.7 (does every automorphism of $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ extend to $K_\Gamma$, uniquely?): the
automorphism case of \cref{opa:as:q:smallparts}. Uniqueness is answered by
\cref{opa:cm:prop:unique}; existence stays open.
\item 12.8 (a kernel-checked automatic-strongness theorem): \cref{opa:as:q:formal}.
\end{itemize}
One question is new:

\begin{question}[Minimal closure for algebraic detection \src{14}]\label{opa:cm:q:closure}
Can the countable detector property be weakened to a necessary and sufficient closure condition
on a subfield $K'$ for the product detection of \cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace}(a)? Which natural
computable or transseries subfields satisfy that condition?
\end{question}
The induced-matching proof needs one all-ones detector for a subsequence of each bad
configuration, not every countable series with arbitrary coefficients, so a closure principle
could quantify over bad support configurations rather than over all increasing sequences. The
question has algebraic and computability aspects: the existence of a detector need not give an
algorithm that recognizes or constructs it from a chosen representation \src{14}.
\Cref{opa:cm:ex:puiseux} shows that some closure condition is needed \mergetag.

\subsection{Stale and checked statements}\label{opa:cm:sub:stale}

\begin{itemize}[itemsep=2pt]
\item \emph{Stale novelty.} 14's abstract says that ``the strong-additivity hypothesis can be
removed'' from this report's real stabilizer classification, its introduction that it
``supplies a missing implication'', and its questions section that its main result ``closes
the automatic-strongness question for the real omnific pair''. At its pin \texttt{3d40856}
this was accurate: \cref{opa:q:strong} was open. At the merge it is stale: sources 12 and 13
answered \cref{opa:q:strong} in \texttt{20c4c9f} (\cref{opa:as:thm:main}). 14 is recorded as an
independent third derivation, and its priority statement is not printed as current; its own
caveat that global priority is not certified is kept.
\item \emph{Checked true.} 14's description of the pinned report (the criterion concerns strong
coefficient-fixing $1$-automorphisms; reconstruction and criterion are credited), the example
$\Frac(\Z\oplus t^{-1}\R[t^{-1}])=\R(t)$ for $\Gamma=\Z$, and the finite checks of its suite, rerun for the
merge (\cref{opa:app:checks}). No mathematical error was found in 14.
\item \emph{Not rechecked.} 14's pinpoint citations of \cite{KS} (an example of a non-strongly
additive automorphism) and of \cite{BCJS} (Theorem~3, Lemmas~4.1--4.2) are reported as 14 gives
them.
\end{itemize}

14's limitations are listed with the other sources' in \cref{opa:app:nonclaims}.

\section{Provenance ledger}\label{opa:app:provenance}

\subsection{Section by section}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.2\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.72\textwidth}}
\toprule
Section & Sources and choices\\
\midrule
\endhead
\ref{opa:sec:intro} & Structure from 09; imported results from all three
automorphism sources; the Fact~4.2 recheck is the merge's (\cref{opa:rem:import}).
10's provenance and symbols were added with it (batch 28); item (8) of
\cref{opa:sub:results} and the rows and paragraph for 11, 12 and 13 in
\cref{opa:sub:provenance} were added in batch 30; item (9), the row and paragraph for 14,
and the counts on the title page and in the abstract were updated in batch 31.\\
\ref{opa:sec:prelim} & 04's one-shift lemma and coefficient polynomial; 09's and 04's
ring decompositions; 04's and 08's divisible part. Units and constant products are
cited from the Diophantine report, not reprinted. 10's one-shift lemma is
\cref{opa:lem:shift}.\\
\ref{opa:sec:criterion} & The criterion is printed once. \emph{Choice:} 09's
generality ($k$ of characteristic zero, unital $\oo$) with 04's import-free proof; the
first implication is 09's, which removes 04's use of $\bigcap n\oo=0$. 09's derivation
form is printed as \cref{opa:thm:derivation}; its operator form, which uses
\cref{opa:fact:import}, as \cref{opa:cor:operator}. \Cref{opa:ex:functionals} is from
the placement dossier's check. \Cref{opa:sc:sub:supportcut} is 10's: its
support-cut form printed as further equivalent conditions, its
least-forbidden-shift proof as a marked second route, necessity without
strongness, and Archimedean blocks.\\
\ref{opa:sec:shears} & Shears from 04 and 09 (after \lbl{saut:thm:shiftflow}); exact
displacement from 04 (08 and 09 have the same computation); 09's uniform shifts;
04's common-shift correspondence as a second, proved route; 08's divisibility-free
threshold as a marked case; the translation example from 04, 08, 09 and the surcomplex
report; rank dichotomy from 04 and 09 (08's for single flows); bottom subgroup from 04;
phase shears from 08. \Cref{opa:sc:sub:twists} is 10's: twists with a general
profile, the binomial family, their fixed fields, finite orbits and algebraic
independence; 10's rank witness and divisibility example are recorded in
\cref{opa:sc:rem:rankdiv}.\\
\ref{opa:sec:factorization} & Factorization from 04 and 09 (credited to \cite{KS} and
\lbl{saut:thm:decomp}); all pair automorphisms from 08 and 09 through
\lbl{odg:def:thm:autreal}. 10 re-derives both.\\
\ref{opa:sec:group} & Bracket law from 04, 08, 09; filtration, rank bound, balanced
brackets, mixed-parameter lemma and sharpness from 09; 04's commutator formula and
08's faithful $\Z\wr\Z$ printed as one group in 04's normalization; the all-times
remark is the merge's. \Cref{opa:sc:ex:threescales} is 10's.\\
\ref{opa:sec:fixed} & Fixed field $\R$ from 04, 08, 09 (explicit choice of 08, 09);
separation, hull, monomial parameters and closure from 04; $\R^\kappa$ and
nonsolvability from 09. \Cref{opa:sc:sub:dcl} is 10's.\\
\ref{opa:sec:remember} & Reconstruction and definitions of $\Z$ and $\Pi$ from 04, 08, 09
are cited to the Diophantine report; printed extras: 08's order formula, the
projections (09), floor (08), truncation and the set-sized version (09).\\
\ref{opa:sec:nondef} & Set-parameter nondefinability from 04, 08, 09 printed once with
04's strengthening; representative rules from 09; twists, one-term class and $\RV$
from 04; contrasts added by the merge. \Cref{opa:sc:sub:twoterm} is 10's; the
outer bound in \cref{opa:sc:cor:innerbound} and the remark on
\lbl{cor:question54} are the merge's.\\
\ref{opa:sec:complex} & Complex criterion and conjugation-compatible witnesses from 09
and 04; real axis inside the kernel from 04; wild lift and Gaussian fixed field from
08 and 09 (09's definition of $\C$ is cited to the Diophantine report);
\cref{opa:sc:prop:complex} from 10.\\
\ref{opa:sec:scope} & Status records and questions: 04's four, 08's first and third,
09's nonstrong question (merged with 04's first), and the merge's non-divisibility
question. 10's four questions: nonstrong maps and generation are merged into
\cref{opa:q:strong,opa:q:generation}, definable closure is recorded under
\cref{opa:q:relative}, and other integer parts is \cref{opa:sc:q:otherparts};
the item on \lbl{dsn:q:languages} and the correction of 10's novelty statement
are the merge's.\\
\cref{opa:part:par} & Manuscript 02 throughout, with credits to
\lbl{bst:thm:fieldcriterion}, \lbl{bst:prop:localdensity}, \lbl{osq:prop:classder} and
\lbl{odg:thm:fractions}; \cref{opa:par:sec:relation} is the merge's.\\
\Cref{opa:part:as} & Manuscripts 11, 12 and 13 (batch 30). \ref{opa:as:sec:intro}: the merge's.
\ref{opa:as:sec:detect}--\ref{opa:as:sec:factor}: 12's spine with 13's binary witness (second
route), set-pair isomorphisms, coefficient matrix and \cref{opa:as:ex:ctnotOz}; the
reconstruction is cited, not reprinted; the convex criterion reproved by 12 and 13 is
\cref{opa:thm:criterion,opa:sc:thm:supportcut}; \cref{opa:as:cor:invisible} is the merge's.
\ref{opa:as:sec:adjoint}: 12's and 13's adjoint theory, 12's general retraction and tower law,
13's kernel statement. \ref{opa:as:sec:gap}--\ref{opa:as:sec:model}: 11's, with the embeddings of
12 and 13 as the instances of \cref{opa:as:thm:explicit}, 12's image corollary (extended to all
three by the merge), 13's non-homogeneity; the Taylor automorphism is \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow}
(credit added for 12 and 13); the gap $C_\omega$ in \cref{opa:as:lem:gaps} is the merge's.
\ref{opa:as:sec:complex}: 12 and 13 for automorphisms, 11 for embeddings.
\ref{opa:as:sec:bound}: 12's regular cardinals, 13's small integer parts, both sources'
distinctions. \ref{opa:as:sec:questions}: the thirty questions of the three sources (ten each)
merged into eighteen, with 12's and 13's admissibility question recorded under
\cref{opa:q:characters}; the status records and the note on the large-cardinal report are the
merge's. Batch 31: 14 added to the sources of the results it re-derives, pointers to
\cref{opa:cm:part}, and 14's comments and status notes after
\cref{opa:as:q:gaussian,opa:as:q:targettests,opa:as:q:classdual,opa:as:q:singular,opa:as:q:otherrings,opa:as:q:smallparts,opa:as:q:formal}.\\
\cref{opa:cm:part} & Manuscript 14 (batch 31). \ref{opa:cm:sec:intro}: the merge's, from 14's
comparison with the report. \ref{opa:cm:sec:matching}: 14's matching lemma and detection proof
(third route), its cancellation example and topology remark. \ref{opa:cm:sec:workspaces}: 14's
definition and proposition; the Puiseux example, the weakened hypothesis after the proposition and
\cref{opa:cm:rem:kappa} are the merge's. \ref{opa:cm:sec:further}: 14's formal evaluation, matrix
formula, $\Gamma=\R$ remark, shear and truncation example and Gaussian statements, translated;
\cref{opa:cm:prop:unique} is the merge's. \ref{opa:cm:sec:questions}: 14's eight questions, one
new, seven merged, and the stale-novelty record.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\subsection{Printed once, and where the merge chose}

\begin{itemize}[itemsep=2pt]
\item \emph{The classification} (04 thm.~criterion, 08 thm.~threshold, 09
thms.~derivation and stabilizer): \cref{opa:thm:criterion} with 04's proof,
\cref{opa:thm:derivation}, \cref{opa:cor:operator}, and 08's
\cref{opa:thm:threshold} as the divisibility-free single-flow case. 04's
$a_{\sigma,\delta}$ and 09's $c_\delta$ are kept as different functionals with
equivalent conditions (\cref{opa:ex:functionals}).
\item \emph{Rank triviality} (04, 09; 08 for single flows): \cref{opa:thm:rank}.
\emph{Common fixed field $\R$} (04, 08, 09): \cref{opa:thm:fixed}. \emph{Set-parameter
nondefinability} (04, 08, 09): \cref{opa:thm:parameters}.
\item \emph{The metabelian non-nilpotent group}: 04's $\langle A_s,B_u\rangle$ and 08's
$\langle A_1,B_1\rangle$ are the same group at $s=u=-1$; printed once as
\cref{opa:thm:commutator,opa:thm:wreath}.
\item \emph{Strong factorization} (04, 09): \cref{opa:thm:factorization}.
\item \emph{Not reprinted} (cited to the Diophantine report): units and constant
products, Pell rigidity, $\Frac\Oz=\No$, the multiplier ring, the reconstruction of
$\R$ and of the order, automorphisms fix $\R$, the split restriction to $\Aut\C$, the
first-order and existential definitions of $\Z$, $\Pi$ and $\C$.
\item \emph{Kept as second routes}: 04's common-shift correspondence
(\cref{opa:thm:lie}) beside the imported one; 04's separation lemma beside the explicit
choice in \cref{opa:thm:fixed}; 09's Pell-divisibility definition of $\Pi_\C$ is
mentioned beside the Diophantine report's.
\item \emph{Added by the merge} (tagged \mergetag, complete proofs): the combined
statement of \cref{opa:thm:criterion}; \cref{opa:ex:functionals};
\cref{opa:rem:alltimes}; \cref{opa:q:nondivisible}; with source 10, the converse in
\cref{opa:sc:thm:twists} and the outer bound in \cref{opa:sc:cor:innerbound}.
\item \emph{Source 10, printed once} (batch 28): its criterion (10 thm.~criterion,
cor.~logcut) as \cref{opa:thm:criterion} with the further equivalent conditions of
\cref{opa:sc:thm:supportcut}; its rank theorem as \cref{opa:thm:rank}; its
stabilizer splitting as \cref{opa:thm:factorization}; its fixed field for
$F=\exp\xi$ as \cref{opa:thm:fixedshear}; its parameter theorem as
\cref{opa:thm:parameters} (with the two-term witness added as
\cref{opa:sc:thm:twoterm}); monomials and the omega-map in its nondefinability
theorem as \cref{opa:thm:parameters,opa:cor:puring}; its inner-support bound as a
consequence of \cref{opa:thm:hull}; its Gaussian theorem as
\cref{opa:thm:complex} with the extras of \cref{opa:sc:prop:complex}; its
nonabelian example beside \cref{opa:thm:commutator}; its one-shift lemma as
\cref{opa:lem:shift}; its floor, fraction-field and coefficient-rigidity
statements as \cref{opa:cor:allautos}, \lbl{odg:thm:fractions},
\lbl{odg:def:thm:multiplier} and \lbl{odg:def:thm:autreal}, not reprinted.
\item \emph{Source 10, kept as a second route}: its least-forbidden-shift proof of
necessity (\cref{opa:sc:thm:supportcut}), beside 04's polynomial argument; its
direct proof of the inner-support bound (\cref{opa:sc:cor:innerbound}).
\item \emph{Sources 12 and 13, printed once} (batch 30): scalar detection (12 thm.~3.3,
13 thm.~3.3) as \cref{opa:as:thm:detect}; the constant-term isomorphism theorem (12
thm.~4.1, 13 thm.~4.1) as \cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso}, with 12's weaker hypothesis; automatic
strongness of $\Aut(\Oz)$ (12 thm.~5.5, 13 thm.~6.1) as \cref{opa:as:thm:main}; the set
pairs (12 cor.~5.6, 13 prop.~5.5 and cor.~6.2) as \cref{opa:as:prop:setpairs,opa:as:cor:setpairs};
monomial determination (12 cor.~5.7, 13 cor.~6.3); the factorization (12 thms.~6.1--6.2, 13
thms.~7.1--7.2) as \cref{opa:as:thm:factor}; scalar representability and adjoints (12
prop.~7.2, thm.~7.3; 13 thms.~8.1, 8.3); the retraction (12 thm.~7.4, 13 prop.~8.4) as
\cref{opa:as:thm:retraction}; monomial adjoints (12 ex.~7.5, 13 ex.~8.5); the class functional
(12 thm.~8.1, 13 ex.~11.2); the surcomplex automorphism theorems (12 thms.~10.1--10.2, 13
thms.~10.1--10.2). Their reprinted reconstruction (12 lems.~5.1--5.2 and
lem.~5.4, prop.~5.3; 13 lems.~5.1--5.2 and prop.~5.3) is cited from the Diophantine report and
\cref{opa:cor:allautos}, and their reproofs of the criterion are the two routes of
\cref{opa:thm:criterion,opa:sc:thm:supportcut}.
\item \emph{Sources 11, 12 and 13, printed once}: the embedding moving a real (11 thm.~6.3,
12 thm.~9.5, 13 thm.~9.5) as \cref{opa:as:thm:explicit}, an instance of 11's classification;
$J(\No)\cap\R=\ker d$ (12, 11 prop.~8.5) as \cref{opa:as:cor:image,opa:as:prop:overlap};
nonelementarity (12 rem.~9.8, 11 thm.~8.3) as \cref{opa:as:thm:elementary}; the surcomplex
embeddings (12 prop.~10.3, 13 cor.~10.4) as the case $\varepsilon=1$ of
\cref{opa:as:thm:complexembed}. 11's reproof of the Taylor automorphism is
\lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow}; its constants and automorphism rigidity (11 prop.~8.1, cor.~8.2) are
\lbl{odg:def:thm:multiplier}, \lbl{odg:def:cor:internal} and \cref{opa:cor:allautos}.
\item \emph{Kept as a second route} (batch 30): 13's binary finite-row proof of detection beside
12's triangular detector (\cref{opa:as:thm:detect}).
\item \emph{Added by the merge} (batch 30): \cref{opa:as:cor:invisible}, the gap $C_\omega$ for
12's exponent embedding (\cref{opa:as:lem:gaps}), the extension of \cref{opa:as:cor:image} to all
three maps, and the second sentence of \cref{opa:as:q:otherrings}.
\item \emph{Source 14, printed once} (batch 31; its numbering): Thm.~1.1, Thm.~5.3 and Cor.~5.5 as
\cref{opa:as:thm:main,opa:as:cor:setpairs}; Lem.~2.2 as \cref{opa:lem:shift} and
\cref{opa:as:lem:intersection}; Thm.~3.3 and Cor.~3.4 as \cref{opa:as:thm:detect}; Thm.~4.1 and
Cor.~4.2 as \cref{opa:as:thm:ctiso,opa:as:cor:recover}; Props.~5.1--5.2 as the cited
reconstruction and \cref{opa:as:prop:setpairs}; Lem.~5.4 as \lbl{odg:thm:fractions}; Cor.~5.6 as
\cref{opa:as:cor:monodet}; Thms.~6.1--6.2 and Cor.~6.3 as
\cref{opa:as:prop:scalarrep,opa:as:thm:adjoint}; Thm.~7.1 as \cref{opa:as:thm:classdefect};
Thm.~8.1 as \cref{opa:as:thm:factor}; Thm.~8.2 as \cref{opa:thm:criterion}; Cor.~8.3 as
\cref{opa:as:cor:arch}; Prop.~9.1 as the inverse of \cref{opa:sc:ex:ranktwo}; Prop.~10.1 and
Thms.~10.2--10.3 as \cref{opa:as:thm:gaussian,opa:as:thm:conjugation}; Ex.~10.4 as the phase twists
of \lbl{odg:def:lem:twist} (table in \cref{opa:cm:sub:once}).
\item \emph{Source 14, kept as a third route}: its induced-matching proof of detection
(\cref{opa:cm:lem:matching}), beside 12's triangular and 13's binary detectors.
\item \emph{Added by the merge} (batch 31): \cref{opa:cm:ex:puiseux,opa:cm:rem:kappa,opa:cm:prop:unique},
the weakened hypothesis after \cref{opa:cm:prop:workspace}, and the status notes after
\cref{opa:as:q:singular,opa:as:q:smallparts}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Corrections and stale statements}\label{opa:sub:corrections}

\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=2pt]
\item \emph{Divisibility} (09): its remark that divisibility is essential is correct for
$\Ak$ and not for $\mathcal R_\Z$: the translation does not preserve
$\Z+t^{-1}\R[t^{-1}]$ (\cref{opa:rem:divisibility}).
\item \emph{Fact 4.2 of \cite{KKS}} (09): the placement dossier doubted that it states
the bijection; the merge re-read the PDF and found that it does, with only an assertion
for proper classes (\cref{opa:rem:import}). 09's claims $\UU_\Oz=\Exp(\LL_A)$ and the
upper half of ``exactly $r-1$'' in its nonsolvability theorem rest on that assertion
and are stated so (\cref{opa:cor:operator,opa:thm:nonsolvable}).
\item \emph{Stale novelty} (04, 08, 09): their reconstruction, first-order recovery of
$\R$ and definitions of $\Pi$ were written at pins \texttt{fb5c4b53} and
\texttt{befe739b}, before the Diophantine report's reconstruction section was placed;
they are cited, not claimed (\cref{opa:sec:remember}). 04's remark that its
reconstruction is not first order is superseded by \lbl{odg:def:cor:internal}. 08's
definition of $\Pi$ has the structure of \lbl{odg:cor:definablect}, which was in the tree
at 08's pin.
\item \emph{08's Question 2} is answered (\cref{opa:sec:remember}) and dropped.
\item \emph{Credits added}: \lbl{saut:thm:shiftflow} (08 and 09 did not credit it),
\lbl{saut:thm:decomp} (04 and 09 credited only \cite{KS}), the rigidity report's
\lbl{thm:no} and \lbl{cor:question54} (09 cited only \cite[Proposition 5.2]{KKS}),
\lbl{bst:thm:fieldcriterion}, \lbl{bst:prop:localdensity}, \lbl{osq:prop:classder} and
\lbl{odg:thm:fractions} (for 02); and the surcomplex report's $T_1(t)=t/(1-t)$ is noted
not to preserve $\Oz$ (\cref{opa:ex:translation}).
\item \emph{Repository statements checked true}: at both pins the catalogue listed 51
reports (04, 08); the fixed-shift flow is \lbl{saut:thm:shiftflow} (04); phase twists
preserve $\Og$ and move the real axis (04); the exponential-rigidity question is answered
in its report (04); Pell rigidity and a definition of $\Z$ were in the Diophantine report
(08); $\Frac\Oz=\No$ was there (09, 02); no report contained an $\Oz$-automorphism
classification (04, 08, 09). 04 reports two date fields for \cite{KKS}~v3 (23 April and
24 August 2026); it is cited as version~3 of 23 April 2026, as elsewhere in the
collection.
\item \emph{Stale novelty} (10): at its pin \texttt{58cd8e1}, which does not contain
this report, 10 stated that no inspected report guide contains the support-cut
criterion and proposed the criterion, the rank boundary, the specialized fixed
fields and the parameter witnesses as new. At the merge these are
\cref{opa:thm:criterion,opa:thm:rank,opa:thm:fixedshear,opa:thm:parameters}; 10 is
credited as an independent fifth source, and only the material listed in
\cref{opa:sub:provenance} is printed as its contribution (\cref{opa:sub:status}).
\item \emph{Checked true} (10): $\Frac\Oz=\No$ is in the Diophantine report
(\lbl{odg:thm:fractions}); the multiplier identity, real-coefficient rigidity and the
Gaussian phase twists recorded in that report's reconstruction audit are now
\lbl{odg:def:thm:multiplier}, \lbl{odg:def:thm:autreal} and \lbl{odg:def:lem:twist};
10 credits them and its own proofs of them are not claimed as new. No
mathematical error was found in 10.
\item \emph{Status notes} (batch 30): \cref{opa:q:strong} is answered by
\cref{opa:as:thm:main}. The statements that recorded it as open or partly answered now
carry status notes: \cref{opa:rem:classified}, \cref{opa:sc:rem:nonstrong}, the
paragraph after \cref{opa:thm:factorization}, \cref{opa:cor:allautos}, the item on
\lbl{osq:q:invisible} in \cref{opa:sub:status}, and the paragraph after
\cref{opa:q:strong}. No label, statement number or earlier text was removed; the non-claims
of 04, 09 and 10 are kept as source records with a status note
(\cref{opa:app:nonclaims}).
\item \emph{A false reason, corrected} (09; merge text of batch 26): after
\cref{opa:cor:gaussianfixed} the report said that a real automorphism ``preserves order and
cannot move an ordinary real''. Read literally this is false: the Taylor automorphism
\lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow} is strong and order preserving and moves a real $b$ to $b+t^\delta$.
The correct reason is that an automorphism preserving $\Oz$ fixes $\R$, which is definable in
the pair. The sentence is corrected in place; no result depended on it, so this is a
correction, not a retraction.
\item \emph{Credits added} (batch 30): \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow} for the Taylor motion
used by 12 and 13, which do not credit it (13's audit calls it an established mechanism);
11 credits it.
\item \emph{Repository statements checked} (batch 30): 12's statement that this report's
Theorem 5.1 is the strong factorization and that its Corollary 5.2 fixes $\R$ and commutes with
the projections and floor without strongness, and that 10's audit disclaims a classification
of nonstrong automorphisms, is accurate at its pin \texttt{a6c68ac}. 13's statements on
\cref{opa:thm:criterion,opa:thm:factorization} and on the Diophantine reconstruction, and 11's
on the surcomplex Taylor motions and exponent lifts, the Diophantine ideal test, multiplier and
fraction field and the automorphism rigidity of this report, are accurate at their pins. Their
statements that no report proves automatic strongness, scalar detection or coefficient-moving
embeddings are still true at the merge.
\item \emph{Wrong statements in delivered files} (batch 30), disclosed and not edited, since
delivered files are kept byte-identical: 11's audit
(\fn{11-coefficient-gaps-SOURCE_AUDIT.md}, Section~2) calls
\texttt{a6c68ac3826ac337762b71a2ef901997d019260c} ``a tree SHA, not a commit SHA''; it is a
commit (it is 12's pin), whose tree is \texttt{12bfef3}. 11's pin \texttt{cf56b89}, its tree
\texttt{21d04b0} and its four blob hashes are correct. 13's audit
(\fn{13-omnific-isomorphisms-SOURCE_AUDIT.md}) gives \texttt{71c1710} as the blob of the
top-level \fn{README.md} at its pin \texttt{0865f04}; that blob is the file at the earlier
commit \texttt{b895e86}, and at \texttt{0865f04} it is \texttt{8c636c7}. Neither affects the
mathematics.
\item \emph{Stale novelty} (14, batch 31): at its pin \texttt{3d40856}, which contains the batch-26
text of this report but not \texttt{20c4c9f}, 14 stated that the strongness hypothesis ``can be
removed'' from this report's real stabilizer classification and that it supplies the missing
implication. That was accurate at the pin and is stale at the merge: sources 12 and 13 answered
\cref{opa:q:strong} in \texttt{20c4c9f}. 14 is credited as an independent third derivation
(\cref{opa:cm:sub:stale}); no mathematical error was found in it.
\item \emph{Delivered-file behaviour} (14, batch 31): 14's script, shipped as
\fn{code/14-automatic-summability-verify.py}, always writes \fn{data/verification.json} under
the parent of its own directory and has no output option; its delivered README says it writes
\fn{data/verification.json}. Run in place it would create an unprefixed
\fn{data/verification.json} in this report; the file is not edited, and the collection README
gives rerun instructions on a copy.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Symbols renamed from the sources}

$\mathcal J$ (08, 09) and $\mathcal I$ (04) are $\Pi$; $B$ (09), $\mathcal A$ (04),
$\mathcal B_k$ (08) are $\Ak$; $A_D$ (09), $O_D$ (04), $A_k$ (08), $R_\Gamma(D,k)$ (02) are
$\Ro$ or $\mathcal R_\oo(k,\Gamma)$, with the coefficient ring $D$ renamed $\oo$ because
$D$ names derivations; $\mathcal P_\Gamma(k)$ (02) is $\Pi_k(\Gamma)$; $H_\delta$ (08) and
$H(a)$ (02) are $C_\delta$ and, in \cref{opa:part:par}, $C(a)$; $\UU_{A_D}$ (09) and $\mathcal U_D(G)$ (04) are
$\UU_{\Ro}(\Gamma)$; $D_{\varphi,\delta}$ (08) is $D_{\delta,-\varphi}$; $q_\eta$ (09),
$[\omega^\beta]\cdot$ (04), $c_a$ (08) and $\chi_b$ (02) are $q_\beta$; 09's $H=\omega^\eta$ and
$H_j$ are $\eta=\omega^\beta$ and $\eta_j$; 08's $A_1,B_1,u,\mathsf E$ are
$A_{-1},B_{-1},u_0,\exp$; 02's covering element $c$ is $z$, its $K_b$ and $H_j$ are
$K_{\mathrm{bd}}$ and $\Gamma_{\le j}$. 08's $\omega$-convention statements are translated to
the $t$-convention (\cref{opa:sub:notation}); \cref{opa:part:par} keeps 02's
large-monomial convention (\cref{opa:par:sub:conv}). Source 10 (large-monomial
convention, $X^g=t^{-g}$, translated): $H_h$ is $C_\delta$ and $C_g$ is $H_g$ (letters
swapped); $\mathrm{pr}_+,\ct,\mathrm{pr}_-$ are $P_-,P_0,P_+$ (subscripts reversed);
$\mathcal I$ is $\Pi$; $R_D$ is $\Ro$; $B$ is $\Ak$; $\mathcal U_D$ is $\UU_{\Ro}(\Gamma)$; the
shift $h$ is $\delta$; $u_g$ is $1+\varepsilon_\sigma(-g)$; $\lambda_b$ is $q_b$; $P$ and $T$
are $\Theta$ and $\xi$; $\sigma_{\lambda,F,h}$ is $\sigma^F_{h,-\lambda}$;
$\mathcal N^\dagger$ is $\mathfrak S^\dagger$ (\cref{opa:sub:notation}).
Sources 11, 12 and 13 (all in the $t$-convention; the table of \cref{opa:as:sub:notation}):
$I_\Gamma,A_G,\mathcal J_\Gamma$ are $\mathcal R_\Gamma$; $A_\Gamma,B_\R,\mathcal A$ are
$\mathcal A_\Gamma$; 12's $\ell_g$ and 13's $L_\gamma$ are $h_\sigma(g)$; 11's $C_\tau$ is
$\gap_\tau$ and its $H$ is $\tau(\Gamma)$, its $\mathcal F$ is $C_1$, its $\Lambda$ is $\Delta$,
its $\Phi_{\tau,\rho}$ is $J_{\tau,\rho}$, its $E_{d,\delta}$ is $\mathcal T_{d,\delta}$, its $S_\tau$
is $M_{1,\tau}$, its $T_h$ is $\iota_\mu$, its $p$ is $\mu_{11}$, its $h_{jk}$ are
$\mu^{\pm\pm}$, its $h_c,h_j,h$ in the parameter theorems are
$\mu^{\mathrm{cmp}},\mu^{\mathrm{jmp}},\mu^{\mathrm{rel}}$, its $\mathcal B_\lambda$ is
$\mathcal V_\lambda$, its $J_\Gamma,L_\Gamma$ are $\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma,K^\C_\Gamma$; 12's
$\theta$ is $\iota_{\mu_{12}}$, its $\psi$ is $\mu_{12}$, its $D$, $\rho_D$ and $J_D$ are $d$,
$\rho_{d,1}$ and $J_{\mu_{12},d}$, its $j^\dagger(1)$ and $E_j$ are $e_\phi$ and $\ret_\phi$, its
$\Lambda$ is $\lambda_{\On}$, its $\mathbf{Sc}$ is $\SC$; 13's second $\tau$ is $\iota_{\mu_{13}}$, its $p$
is $\mu_{13}$, its $\rho=\sigma|_k$ is $\alpha$, its $D$, $E_D$ and $F_D$ are $d$, $\rho_{d,1}$ and
$J_{\mu_{13},d}$, its $T^*$ is $T^\dagger$, its $\lambda$ is $\lambda_{\On}$, its $\mathcal P$ is
$\Pi$, and its factorization $U\circ M$ is $M\circ u$ with $U=MuM^{-1}$. The large-cardinal
report's $J$ is $\hat\jmath$. No normalization changes.
Source 14 (the table of \cref{opa:cm:sub:notation}): its $A_\Gamma=\Z\oplus\Pi$ and $A$ are
$\mathcal R_\Gamma$; its $B$ is $\Ak$ ($\mathcal A_\Gamma$ for $k=\R$); its $\langle x,y\rangle$ is
$\pairing xy$; its coefficient isomorphism $\tau$ is $\alpha$ and its exponent automorphism
$\phi$ is $\tau$; its $\rho,\widetilde\rho$ are $\sigma,\widetilde\sigma$; its $\nu$, $\mathcal U$,
$\mathcal G$, $\mathcal M$ are $u$, $\UU_{\mathcal R_\Z}(\Gamma)$, the stabilizer and the monomial maps;
its $L_\nu(g)$, $a_{\nu,\delta}$ are $h_u(g)$, $a_{u,\delta}$; its $T^*$ is $T^\dagger$; its $\Lambda$
is $\lambda_{\On}$; its power series $F$ is $\mathsf F$ and its subfield $F$ is $K'$; its $P_{<\gamma}$
is $T_{<\gamma}$; its shear letters $e_1,e_2$ are $e_2,e_1$ and its $s$ is $t^{e_1}$; its additive
$h$ is $\theta$; its infinitesimals $u_j$ are $z_j$; its $d>0$ is $\delta$; its $\mathbf{No}[i]$, $\mathbf{Oz}[i]$, $A_\C$, $K_\C$, $\Pi_\C$, $B_\C$ are $\SC$, $\Og$,
$\mathcal R^\C_\Gamma$, $K^\C_\Gamma$, $\Pi^\C_\Gamma$, $\C\oplus\Pi^\C_\Gamma$; its target monomial $u$ is
$t$. No normalization changes.

\section{Non-claims, by source}\label{opa:app:nonclaims}

No source claims to solve a named conjecture, and none claims Lean verification, peer
review or historical priority; the finite suites check identities only. The report
keeps every limitation stated by each source.

\paragraph{Source 09.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item The classification is only within strong, coefficient-fixing,
leading-term-preserving automorphisms; it does not assert that an abstract
automorphism preserving $\Ro$ is strong, and nonstrong pair automorphisms are not
classified (the definability recovery applies to them; the logarithmic classification
does not).
\item Coefficient functionals come from an existing strong derivation; arbitrary
functions satisfying the vanishing condition are not asserted to assemble into an
operator.
\item The shears are plain-field automorphisms and are not claimed to preserve the
Gonshor exponential.
\item No set or NBG class of class-function graphs is formed; actions are encoded by
single class relations; supports are sets.
\item The exponential--logarithm correspondence and the factorization pattern are
imported, not reproved or claimed; the explicit many-shift constructions avoid the
class version of the import.
\item No new factorization theorem for omnific integers is claimed; \cite{LM} addresses a
different problem.
\item $\Oz$ is not identified with a model of full ordinary arithmetic.
\item Truncation at a named monomial is not a uniform definable choice of boundary.
\item Nondefinability is relative to the field-and-integer-part language; Conway normal
forms remain set-theoretically defined.
\item Hahn-sum formulas are not claims of convergence in the fine topology.
\item Modulus equivariance is not a claim that moduli are fixed.
\item The repository comparison was bounded and targeted; search coverage is not a
proof of novelty.
\item The exact-arithmetic program checks finite identities only; it does not decide
well-ordering, prove the correspondence, verify class-theoretic statements or certify
novelty; the formalization order is a proposal, not implemented modules.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 04.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item It does not classify abstract (nonstrong) automorphisms of $\Oz$; the fraction-field
extension of an abstract automorphism is not known to be strong.
\item It imposes no exponential, omega-map, birthday or simplicity structure, and does not
revisit the collection's exponential-rigidity question as if unanswered.
\item The criterion classifies existing maps; it is not a construction for arbitrary
logarithmic characters, and the convex condition alone is not asserted to solve global
admissibility.
\item The relative fixed field is computed for the elementary subgroup only; the full
stabilizer only for monomial parameters; the Hahn hull is not asserted to be definable
closure.
\item Class maps are treated individually; no class of all automorphisms is formed.
\item Nothing is claimed after naming a proper class of parameters.
\item Bare nondefinability of coefficient-one monomials already follows from character
twists; the nondefinability statement alone is not advertised as a new mechanism.
\item $\Frac\Oz=\No$ is a full-class statement; a set-sized Hahn field need not be the
fraction field of its integer-part ring.
\item Its intrinsic coefficient invariance was not claimed to be first order (now
superseded by the Diophantine report).
\item Real and imaginary shear times preserve different structures; its Gaussian result is
not the first surcomplex automorphism moving the real axis.
\item No generation or density theorem; fine-topology convergence is not strong summation.
\item The fixed-shift exponential, the general monomial/kernel decomposition and the
exponential--derivation correspondence are prior work.
\item Its finite checks are not proofs and do not implement arbitrary normal forms; no Lean
file, no repository checkout or build; historical priority unverified.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 08.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item The threshold covers the elementary family only; rank-one rescalings and characters
preserve the integer part; not every leading-term-preserving automorphism is claimed to be
the exponential of one fixed-shift derivation.
\item Finite-dimensional phase images are a sufficient condition only.
\item No first-order Gaussian reconstruction was claimed (one now exists,
\cref{opa:sec:remember}).
\item The automorphisms are not asserted to commute with the surreal exponential, the
Berarducci--Mantova derivation or the omega-map; the wreath product does not contradict
rigidity for those structures.
\item Parameter avoidance fails for set-sized fields with the whole field named; a
universe-relative collection is not identified with the proper class.
\item No classification of the full stabilizer, and no solution of additional-structure
problems.
\item The reconstruction is not a homomorphism $\Oz\to\R$.
\item The nondefinability is not a statement about adding an arbitrary class predicate, nor
about automorphisms required to commute with the omega-map.
\item Strong summation is not topological convergence.
\item Pell rigidity, the existence of nontrivial Hahn automorphisms, the
derivation--exponential construction and the monomial-character viewpoint are not claimed
new; some elementary lemmas may be known; priority is provisional.
\item Its questions are not named historical problems.
\item No Lean; not refereed; the checks are regression tests, not proofs of well-ordering,
parameter avoidance or definability.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 02.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item Root-covered is sufficient, not claimed necessary; failure of the monomial criterion
is not used to claim failure of cancellation or rigidity.
\item The finite-type theorems are theorems about sets; no $\operatorname{Spec}(\Oz)$ and no
class compactness; ``$\Aut(\Oz)$'' and $\ML$ of class rings are shorthand; the finite-type
theorem is not a statement that a class cannot inject into a set.
\item It does not classify $\Aut(\Oz)$, all derivations, or multivariable polynomial
automorphisms, and makes no Jacobian-conjecture claim.
\item Not every homomorphism into every target is constant: injectivity and domain targets
are required; no noninjective analogue is given.
\item It does not claim that $R[\mathbf T]$ has no locally nilpotent derivation; a Hahn
expansion is not a grading.
\item The orbit-field formula concerns monomials; only a lower bound holds for the full
formal image.
\item Formal flows involve no analytic convergence and no fine-topology limits, $s$ is not a
surreal, and they are not exponentiation inside a Hahn field.
\item The cancellation theorem does not solve the unrestricted cancellation problem;
$\ML(R[\mathbf T])=R$ is not a solution of a general stable-rigidity conjecture.
\item Not new: character twists \cite{KKS}, strong-invariance terminology \cite{Hirano}, the
Makar--Limanov framework \cite{CD}, Euler derivations, Vandermonde arguments, and the
example of \cite{LM}.
\item No named factorization conjecture is claimed solved; the literature search is not a
priority certificate.
\item The questions are not presented as published open problems; no Lean, no peer review;
the checks are finite identities.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 10.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item The classification is within strong, coefficient-fixing, leading-term-fixing
automorphisms; nonstrong and coefficient-moving automorphisms are not classified,
and strongness is not a harmless omitted hypothesis.
\item The criterion classifies existing strong maps; an abstract character obeying the
cut condition is not asserted to give an automorphism, since summability and an
inverse must be proved.
\item The rank theorem concerns the leading-term-fixing factor only; it does not say
that all rank-one automorphisms are trivial, and monomial maps such as
$t^\gamma\mapsto t^{2\gamma}$ and character rescalings are not counterexamples.
Archimedean rank is not the order-unit condition of the holonomic report.
\item The strong valued decomposition, principal-unit characters and their crossed
law \cite{KS}, simplicity rigidity \cite{KKS}, the existence of nontrivial
strongly linear $1$-automorphisms, Hahn normal forms and the support description
of $\Oz$ \cite{LM} are prior work; the splitting is not a new decomposition
theorem for $\Aut(\No)$.
\item Not every truncation is preserved, only signed Archimedean blocks.
\item The fixed field is relatively, not absolutely, algebraically closed; no exact
transcendence degree of a class extension is claimed.
\item The nonabelian example gives no presentation or exhaustive description of the
stabilizer; no transfinite composition or closure theorem is claimed.
\item No proper-class-indexed sum is formed and no class of automorphisms is
formed; class maps are individual, in NBG with global choice.
\item The bounded-birthday automorphism does not preserve birthdays or the simplicity
hierarchy globally.
\item The definable-closure statements are upper bounds, not descriptions of
definable closure and not quantifier elimination; they do not recover every
coefficient from the projections.
\item The formal exponential is not the Gonshor exponential.
\item $\Frac\Oz=\No$, the multiplier identity and coefficient rigidity are prior
repository results, credited and not claimed; automatic fixing of coefficients is
asserted only for the full surreal field, not for arbitrary $\Ro\subseteq K$.
\item $\Ro$ is called integer-part-shaped, not an integer part in every generality;
no ordering of the complex field is asserted and no global surcomplex exponential
is introduced.
\item The divisibility example is for $\oo=k$ and does not assert that translation
preserves $\Z+t^{-1}\R[t^{-1}]$; the criterion is not transferred to other integer
parts.
\item Initial subdomains, truncation-closed subrings and subrings stabilized by an
automorphism are different notions, and none is substituted for another.
\item It does not answer the omega-map question of \cite{KKS}, factorization problems
for omnific integers, or the holonomic order-unit question; no named published
conjecture is solved, and its questions are research questions.
\item Its finite checks do not prove summability, well-ordering of arbitrary supports,
the existence of separating functionals, class-size statements or
nondefinability.
\item No Lean, no repository clone or build, no peer review; novelty is provisional,
and the repository and literature comparison was targeted, not exhaustive.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 11.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item It is an unrefereed research draft with no Lean verification; priority is
provisional and the novelty assessment limited; the existence of proper Hahn-field embeddings
is not claimed as new.
\item The classification carries the exact-monomial hypothesis: it is not a classification of
all surreal embeddings, does not assert that omnific-preserving embeddings are strong, and does
not cover characters or unit corrections.
\item Taylor automorphisms, exponent lifts, the reconstruction of constants and the classical
inputs (Hahn fields, normal forms, real and algebraic closedness, quantifier elimination,
extension of derivations) are prior work.
\item The derivations of $\R$ are choice-dependent and noncomputable.
\item ``Monomial-preserving'' does not mean commuting with the omega-map, and ``strong'' does
not include $\R$-linearity; no omega-map or exponential question of \cite{KKS} is claimed,
and the explicit map fails omega-map compatibility at $1$.
\item Elementarity of pure-field embeddings is classical; fixing the coefficients is not proved
sufficient for elementarity of the pair.
\item The continuum of conjugacy types is a lower bound, not a classification.
\item No nonconjugacy is claimed for automorphisms preserving $\Og$ but not conjugation, and
non-conjugation-compatible surcomplex embeddings are not classified.
\item $\Frac\mathcal R_\Gamma=K_\Gamma$ is not assumed for set-sized $\Gamma$.
\item No collection of class automorphisms or of open subclasses is formed; the topological
claims are local.
\item The annulus bounds are external.
\item The finite tests prove no infinite, class, conjugacy or model-theoretic statement.
\item No repository was modified or built.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 12.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item It is unrefereed, has no Lean verification, solves no named published conjecture and
certifies no priority; the repository comparison was limited to four files and five public
sources, the full articles and the ledger were not read, and a local clone failed.
\item Support duality and finiteness spaces are not new; $\Frac\Oz=\No$ is classical; the
reconstruction and the convex criterion are prior repository results.
\item Admissibility is retained: arbitrary coefficient arrays are not claimed to define
automorphisms.
\item The proper embeddings are not a counterexample to strongness of all embeddings, and are not
claimed initial, exponential or omega-compatible; the derivation is not computable.
\item The adjoint theorem is for set-sized groups only, fails for $\No$, and does not apply to
non-$\R$-linear maps.
\item No unrestricted Gaussian valuation-preservation theorem is proved, and preservation of the
real axis is not assumed.
\item The support-bound result is for regular uncountable $\kappa$ only; no singular case, and no
identification with a birthday cutoff.
\item $\ret_\phi$ is not a field retraction or a positive conditional expectation.
\item No preservation of the exponential, logarithm, omega-map, birthdays or simplicity is
claimed.
\item Invariance under automorphisms is not first-order definability.
\item The set-pair corollary does not assert that automorphisms of $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ extend
when $\Frac\mathcal R_\Gamma$ is smaller.
\item Omnific-preserving automorphisms are not claimed to be trivial.
\item The distinction from the three-duals report is not an exhaustive non-overlap claim.
\item Finite checks are not proofs.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 13.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item It is AI-assisted and unrefereed, not proof-assistant checked, not a resolution of a named
conjecture, and makes no priority claim; a failed search is not novelty, and it does not claim to
settle every question posed.
\item Earlier repository results are credited: fractions, reconstruction, the strong stabilizer
and the shears.
\item The scalar strong-dual representation is set-size only; the automorphism theorem is not.
\item The residue criterion is not valuation-topological convergence and not first-order
definability of summation.
\item Not all automorphisms of $\No$ are strong (the examples of \cite{KKS} stand), and omnific
automorphisms are not trivial.
\item Local convex vanishing is not proved sufficient, and no class-wide operator
exponential--logarithm correspondence is used.
\item It does not prove that every automorphism of $\Og$ is valuation-compatible, and conjugation
is not intrinsic; its two Gaussian theorems must not be merged into an unconditional
classification.
\item The full-pair theorem assumes an ambient isomorphism; isomorphisms of the small integer parts
are not claimed to extend.
\item No Gonshor exponential, Berarducci--Mantova derivation or omega-map is used; its exponent
map is not a field map; no proper-class Zorn lemma or basis is used.
\item The drift embedding comes with no uniqueness claim; the failure of homogeneity concerns
set-sized induced substructures, not elementary maps.
\item Finite checks do not verify infinite summability, transcendence bases or surjectivity.
\item No repository build was run, and the formalization ledger was not read.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 14.}
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=1pt]
\item It is an AI-assisted research draft, not refereed and not Lean-verified; ``theorem'' means
that a proof is supplied. Global priority is not certified: the literature search is not a
certificate of absence, and the priority of the omnific application, of the detector's
formulation and of the class counterexample needs independent review.
\item Finiteness-space duality \cite{BCJS} and the general decompositions of Hahn-field
automorphisms \cite{KS,KKS} are prior work; no priority is claimed for those frameworks, and the
underlying support duality is not claimed to originate there. The reconstruction and the
convex-scale criterion are credited to this report and re-proved.
\item The unrestricted Gaussian problem is not solved: neither automatic strongness of every
automorphism preserving $\Og$ nor a nonstrong counterexample is claimed, and the phase example is
strong, so it disproves nothing.
\item No embedding theorem: the isomorphism proof uses surjectivity essentially, and no statement
about constant-term-compatible embeddings is inferred.
\item No compatibility with the global Gonshor exponential is claimed; the formal exponential and
logarithm are evaluated at infinitesimals only. Continuity, the Conway monomial cross-section and
arbitrary truncations are neither assumed nor concluded, and commutation with every truncation is
false.
\item The representation of strong functionals and the adjoint theory hold for set-sized exponent
groups only and fail for $\No$.
\item It does not assert that every ring automorphism of every $\mathcal R_\Gamma$ extends to its
full Hahn field; the fraction-field argument is for $\Oz$ only.
\item The convex criterion classifies existing normalized automorphisms; it does not construct
automorphisms from additive data satisfying the vanishing condition, since joint support conditions
and invertibility are needed.
\item Summability, the monomials and a cross-section are not claimed first-order definable; the
intersection formula for $\Pi$ is not a single first-order formula; the conclusion is invariance of
an externally quantified operation, not definability.
\item The class-group notation $\Aut(\No,\Oz)$ and its factorization are elementwise; no NBG class of
proper-class graphs is formed; every summed family is set-indexed and every series has set support,
in NBG with global choice.
\item The countable detector property is a sufficient closure condition only; it is not claimed for
transseries, Puiseux or left-finite subfields.
\item The adjoints are for a bilinear algebraic pairing, not a Hilbert-space inner product;
$\sigma^\dagger=\sigma^{-1}$ is not analytic unitarity, and the pairing is not a Hermitian norm.
\item Formal summability is not convergence of partial sums or in the valuation topology; no
ordering of the index set is used; the automorphism--derivation correspondence of \cite{BKKPS} is
not used.
\item Its finite checks validate finite identities only: they do not establish well-ordering of
arbitrary supports, quantify over all series, verify class replacement or prove the main theorem.
No Lean compilation was performed, and the proposed Lean modules and names are plans only.
\item Conway's irreducibility conjectures are not addressed and no famous open conjecture is
claimed settled; its questions are proposed research problems, not published open problems.
\item The repository was read, not modified; its comparison was targeted, not an audit of every
manuscript; the Wikipedia article it lists was an orientation source only.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Status note} (batch 30). Non-claim~1 of 09, non-claim~1 of 04 and non-claim~1 of 10 say
that nonstrong automorphisms are not classified and that strongness is not shown. They are
kept as the sources' records. For $(\No,\Oz)$ and the full real Hahn pairs with divisible
exponent group, \cref{opa:as:thm:main,opa:as:cor:setpairs} now show that every automorphism is
strong, so \cref{opa:as:thm:factor} classifies them all; for $\Og$ without conjugation or
valuation data, and for a general $(k,\oo)$, those non-claims still describe the state of
knowledge (\cref{opa:as:q:gaussian,opa:as:q:otherrings}).

Counts: 13 non-claims from 09, 13 from 04, 12 from 08, 11 from 02, 18 from 10, 13 from 11,
14 from 12, 12 from 13, 16 from 14.

\section{Finite checks}\label{opa:app:checks}

Nine suites ship with the report, byte-identical to their delivery, under the prefixes
of their manuscripts. They check finite identities in exact arithmetic; they do not
prove Hahn summability, class-sized statements, definability or novelty. They were
rerun on copies for the merges (10's for the batch-28 addition, 11's, 12's and 13's for
the batch-30 addition, 14's for the batch-31 addition) with Python~3.14.4 and
SymPy~1.14.0, with the recorded results reproduced.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.33\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.59\textwidth}}
\toprule
Script and record & Content and behaviour\\
\midrule
\endhead
\fn{code/09-omnific-preserving-verify_finite_identities.py};
\fn{data/09-omnific-preserving-verification_output.txt} & Standard library. Product rule
and bracket on finite Laurent polynomials, balanced brackets for ranks 2 to 8, mixed
group-commutator coefficients with square-zero parameters, shear iterates, exponential
laws, the rank-one escape, Pell growth. Twelve PASS lines and ``ALL CHECKS PASSED''.
Prints only.\\
\fn{code/04-preserving-automorphisms-verification.py};
\fn{data/04-preserving-automorphisms-verification_report.json} & SymPy~1.14.0
(\fn{data/04-preserving-automorphisms-requirements.txt}). 7{,}062 assertions in 17 groups:
exponential group law, bracket formula, coefficient polynomial, first corrections,
commutator coefficients in lexicographic $\Q^3$. \emph{Writes} its report to
\fn{--output}, by default \fn{verification_report.json} in the working directory.\\
\fn{code/08-integer-part-symmetries-verify.py};
\fn{data/08-integer-part-symmetries-verification.json} & Standard library. 9{,}469 checks in
15 categories: derivation rule, falling-factorial iterates, flow composition, bracket,
Vandermonde determinants, the semidirect-product law, commutator phases. \emph{Always
writes} \fn{verification.json} beside itself.\\
\fn{code/02-parameter-rigidity-verify.py};
\fn{data/02-parameter-rigidity-verification.json} & Standard library. 7{,}041 assertions in 13
families: Euler product rule, factorial Leibniz, truncated flow composition, commutation,
geometric remainders, Vandermonde determinants, binomial identity. Writes
\fn{verification.json} beside itself unless \fn{--output} is given.\\
\fn{code/10-support-cut-verify.py};
\fn{data/10-support-cut-verification.json} & Standard library, exact rational
arithmetic. 28{,}668 assertions in 19 categories: binomial coefficients, formal
additive composition and inverses by two independent power algorithms, rank-two
composition, inverse and multiplicativity, signed support projections, exact first
displacement, the least-bad-shift coefficient and the rational root crossing zero,
the three-scale commutator. Writes \fn{verification.json} beside itself unless
\fn{--output} is given.\\
\fn{code/11-coefficient-gaps-verify_finite_models.py};
\fn{data/11-coefficient-gaps-verification.json} & Standard library, exact fractions, seed
20260923. 3{,}880 assertions in 15 categories: truncated Taylor identities over
$\Q[b,b^{-1}]$, multiplication of finite outer Hahn polynomials with lexicographic shifts,
Taylor inversion, integer-part preservation and reflection, signs of negative blocks, rational
samples of the index maps, parameter fixing. Prints to standard output; writes a file only
with \fn{--output}.\\
\fn{code/12-automatic-strongness-verify.py};
\fn{data/12-automatic-strongness-verification.json} & Standard library, exact fractions,
seed 20260923. 17{,}774 assertions: 10{,}800 triangular-detector checks, 4{,}502 monomial
adjoint and transfer checks, 2{,}472 rank-two Taylor-block and binomial checks. \emph{Writes}
\fn{verification.json} beside itself unless \fn{--output} is given.\\
\fn{code/13-omnific-isomorphisms-verify.py};
\fn{data/13-omnific-isomorphisms-verification.json} & Standard library, exact fractions,
seed 20260923. 13{,}885 assertions in 16 categories: binary finite-row choices and their
persistence, descending-support detection, coefficient reconstruction, monomial adjoints and
retractions, scale-separated Taylor models. \emph{Writes} \fn{../data/verification.json}
relative to its own directory unless \fn{--output} is given.\\
\fn{code/14-automatic-summability-verify.py};
\fn{data/14-automatic-summability-verification.json} & Standard library, exact fractions,
seed 20260923 for the adjoint trials. Generalized binomial identities to degree 20 for 31
rational parameters (961 multiplication and 31 inverse cases); the induced matching on a
banded 256-row prefix (rows meeting columns $n,n+1,n+3$), with a matching of size 64 and its
all-ones detector; the sign-reversed transpose \eqref{opa:cm:eq:adjointmatrix} in 100 random
trials with 13 source and 9 target exponents; the finite prefixes of \cref{opa:cm:ex:cancellation} for 1, 2, 5,
16 and 64 pairs. Prints its report and \emph{always writes} \fn{../data/verification.json}
relative to its own directory; it has no output option.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The placement dossiers also recorded independent checks (281 for the automorphism
cluster, including \cref{opa:ex:functionals}, the commutator formula for symbolic $s,u$,
the leading terms of \cref{opa:thm:commutator} for $n\le4$, the conjugation lemma and the
failure example of \cref{opa:rem:divisibility}; for 02 the quotient-rule identity of
\cref{opa:par:ex:flow} to 29 terms; and 8{,}724 assertions for sources 11--13, including
the Taylor-block embedding in a lexicographic $\Z^2$ model, the failure of the ambient
Taylor automorphism to preserve $\Oz$ (\cref{opa:as:sub:taylor}), the binary finite-row algorithm over $\mathbb F_2$ and
$\Q$, the triangular detector, the least-forbidden-shift coefficient and
\cref{opa:as:ex:ctnotOz}); these scripts are not shipped.
